-space, while a smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold is a topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold with
additional structure
.

Remark 14
.

We will not state the dimension
n
of a
\langle k\rangle
manifold when it is implied. We declare a countable disjoint union of topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds of varying dimension to be a topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold.

Remark 15
.

Definitions
7
and
10
all have counterparts (
\langle S\rangle
-spaces,
\langle S\rangle
-manifolds) where one uses the power set
2^{S}
to stratify a manifold, for some arbitrary finite set
S
. However, when the set
S
is not specified, we will use the term
\langle k\rangle
-manifold to refer to any one of these objects; thus we may say that
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{1},\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2},\ldots
are all
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds. When
k
is being used to refer to a specific quantity, this should be clear from the context.

Remark 16
.

In keeping with typical notation in differential topology, even though an
\langle S\rangle
-space is a really a pair
(S,X)
, we will often simply refer to the
\langle S\rangle
-space
(S,X)
by
X
while suppressing
S
from the notation. When using this notation, we will use
S_{X}
to refer to the set controlling the stratification of
X
; thus,
X
is an
\langle S_{X}\rangle
-space. In keeping with this notation, we will write
S_{\emptyset}=\bullet
.

Remark 17
.

The definition of a topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
X
forces the underlying stratified space to satisfy condition (
13
). The
X_{j}
are referred to as the
faces
of
X
in
[
29
]
, and as boundary hypesurfaces in
[
12
]
. Any subspace of a topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold with the induced stratification will still satisfy conditoin (
13
); thus, while a previous usage of the term
\langle k\rangle
-space
[
85
]
does not have condition (
13
), the usage of the term is consistent with
[
85
]
.

Remark 18
.

A smooth manifold with corners may be a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold in many different ways. For example, while a closed manifold with boundary
X
is canonically a
\langle 1\rangle
-manifold, it can also be thought of as a
\langle 2\rangle
-manifold in
2
different, by setting either
X(\{1\})=\emptyset
or
X(\{2\})=\emptyset.
We will use this freedom of stratification later in the paper.

4.2
Derived
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds.

As discussed in the introduction, when studying equivariant Morse- and Floer-theory, we are forced to deal with moduli spaces of flows which are not cut out transversely, and are thus no longer manifolds. To deal with this issue, we have to work with
virtually smooth flow categories
. Just as smooth flow categories are flow categories enriched in
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds, virtually smooth flow categories are flow categories enriched in
derived
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds
, which we now describe.

Definition 11
.

A
derived
\langle k\rangle
-manifold

\mathcal{T}
is a triple
(T,V,\sigma)
where

•

T
is a smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold,

•

V
is a vector bundle over
T
, and

\sigma:T\to V
is a continuous section.

The
underlying
\langle k\rangle
-space
of
\mathcal{T}
is
\sigma^{-1}(0)\subset T
with the induced stratification. The space
T
is the
thickening
of
\sigma^{-1}(0)
, with
V
the
obstruction bundle
and
\sigma
the
Kuranishi section
or the
obstruction section
. A
virtual smoothing
of a
\langle k\rangle
-space
X
is a stratum-preserving homeomorphism from the underlying
\langle k\rangle
-space of a derived
\langle k\rangle
-manifold to
X
. Moreover, we require that every connected component of
T(S)
contains a point in
\sigma^{-1}(0)\cap T(S)
.

A
topological derived
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
has the same definition but without the smooth structure on
T
. Similarly, derived
\langle k\rangle
is the same notion but
T
is only required to be a
\langle k\rangle
-space.

Remark 19
.

We will continue to use the linguistic conventions of Remarks
15
and
16
with derived
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds.

When a
\langle k\rangle
-space
X
has a virtual smoothing
\mathcal{T}
, it naturally comes with a series of other virtual smoothings, the
stabilizations
of
\mathcal{T}
, which should be thought of as equivalent to
\mathcal{T}
.

Definition 12
.

A
stabilization
of a derived
\langle k\rangle
manifold
\mathcal{T}=(T,V,\sigma)
is any derived
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
\mathcal{T}_{W}=(W,\pi^{*}_{V}\oplus\pi^{*}W,\pi^{*}\sigma\oplus id)
associated to a vector bundle
\pi:W\to T
, where
W
is given the
\langle k\rangle
-manifold structure
W(S^{\prime})=\pi^{-1}(T(S^{\prime}))
for
S^{\prime}\subset S_{T}
, and
id
denotes the tautological section
W\to\pi^{*}W
.

Remark 20
.

Replacing a virtual smoothing
\mathcal{T}=(T,V,\sigma)
with
\mathcal{T^{\prime}}=(T^{\prime},V_{T^{\prime}},\sigma|_{T^{\prime}})
for
T^{\prime}\subset T
an open subset of
\sigma^{-1}(0)
should also be thought of as an equivalence, and we may perform such replacements during some constructions without explicitly pointing them out.

4.3
Virtually smooth flow categories

Definition 13
.

Let
\langle k\rangle-Spc^{\prime}
be the category with objects all
\langle S\rangle
-spaces (including the empty space, which is a
\langle\bullet\rangle
-space), with morphisms from
(S_{X},X)
to
(S_{Y},Y)
given by pairs
(f,\bar{f})
, where

•

if
S_{X}=\bullet
or
S_{Y}=\bullet
then there are no morphisms, unless
S_{X}=S_{Y}=\bullet
in which case there is the identity morphism. Otherwise,

•

f:S_{X}\to S_{Y}
is an injective map of sets, and

•

\bar{f}:X\to Y
is a map of spaces which is a homeomorphism onto its image
Y(f(S_{X}))
. Moreover,
\bar{f}
induces homeomorphisms
X(S^{\prime})\to Y(f(S^{\prime}))
for
S^{\prime}\subset S_{X}
.

The category
\langle k\rangle-Spc^{\prime}
is symmetric monoidal with product
\times
on nonempty
\langle k\rangle
-spaces given by

((S_{X},X),(S_{Y},Y))\mapsto((S_{X}\cup S_{Y}),X\times Y))

(with the stratification given by the product stratification), extended to include the empty
\langle k\rangle
-space via
\emptyset\times X=X\times\emptyset=\emptyset
in the natural way. The unit object of this monoidal structure is the
\langle 0\rangle
-space with one point.

Definition 14
.

A flow category
\mathcal{C}
is a category enriched in
\langle k\rangle-Spc^{\prime}
such that the endomorpisms of any object are the unit object in
\langle k\rangle-Spc^{\prime}
, and such that the compositions
cover the boundaries of the morphism spaces
, namely that any morphism in
\partial\mathcal{C}(x,y)
, for any pair of distinct objects
(x,y)
of
\mathcal{C}
, is the composition of some pair of morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
. Here, we say that the elements of the
\langle k\rangle
-space
\mathcal{C}(x,y)
are the morphisms of
\mathcal{C}
, and the compositions of morphisms are given by the maps
\bar{f}
in Definition
13
.

Definition 15
.

A
grading
on a flow category
\mathcal{C}
is a map
\mu:Ob(\mathcal{C})\to\mathbb{Z}
. Or pairs of objects
(x,y)
of
\mathcal{C}
, we define

\mu(x,y)=\mu(x)-\mu(y)-1.

(16)

Definition 16
.

An
integral action
on a flow category
\mathcal{C}
is a map
\bar{A}:Ob(\mathcal{C})\to\{n\in\mathbb{Z}:n\geq-1\}

such that for every pair of objects
(x,y)
of
\mathcal{C}
such that
\mathcal{C}(x,y)\neq\bullet
and
x\neq y
, we have

|S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}|=\bar{A}(x)-\bar{A}(y)-1;

together with isomorphisms

\beta_{x,y}:S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}\to[\bar{A}(x)-\bar{A}(y)-1]

such that for the composition map
(f,\bar{f})
where

f:S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}\cup S_{\mathcal{C}(y,z)}\to S_{\mathcal{C}(x,z)}

one has

\beta_{x,z}\circ f\circ\beta_{x,y}^{-1}(a)=a

and

\beta_{x,z}\circ f\circ\beta_{y,z}^{-1}(a)=a+\bar{A}(x)-\bar{A}(y).

(17)

More invariantly, we can think of the integral action as a choice of lift of the sets
S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}
to totally ordered sets such that the maps
f:S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}\cup S_{\mathcal{C}(y,z)}\to S_{\mathcal{C}(x,z)}
associated to composition in
\mathcal{C}
are order-preserving, where we give the domain of
f
the ordering where every element of the first term in the disjoint union is less than every element of the second term.

In particular, there are only morphisms from
x
to
y
if
\bar{A}(x)>\bar{A}(y)
, since these conditions force
S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}
to be equal to
\bullet
if
\bar{A}(x)\leq\bar{A}(y)
.

When
\bar{A}
is an integral action, we write

\bar{A}(x,y)=\bar{A}(x)-\bar{A}(y)-1.

(18)

Remark 21
.

The integral action is a helpful device for organizing the combinatorics of flow categories which arises naturally in existing constructions. Later in the paper, we will be forced to modify flow categories by
changing their integral actions
, modifying the stratifications of the morphism spaces
\mathcal{C}(x,y)
from
\langle k\rangle
-manifold to
\langle k+\ell\rangle
-manifolds, as in Remark
13
.

Remark 22
.

The reason for the condition on the range of
\bar{A}
because of the need to fix such a convention when working with the Floer homotopy type. Note that if
y\in\mathcal{C}
achieves the minimal value
\bar{A}(y)=-1
for the integral action then
\bar{A}(x,y)=\bar{A}(x)
; this is the reason for using this particular convention.

If
\bar{A}
is an integral action then so is
\bar{A}+1
; this operation will turn out not to change the Floer homotopy type up to a canonical weak equivalence.

Remark 23
.

Without loss of generality, we can think of the nonempty
\langle k\rangle
-spaces in a flow category with an integral action as being as satisfying
S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}=[\bar{A}(x,y)]
, with all maps
\beta_{x,y}
of Definition
16
being identity maps. The existence of an integral action it being possible to ehnance the
S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}
to
totally ordered
sets, such that the maps of Definition
13
become maps of
ordered
sets, where we define the monoidal structure on ordered sets to be

(S,T)\mapsto S\cup T,\text{ where }s\leq t\text{ for all }s\in S,t\in T.

Definition 17
.

Given an integral action
\bar{A}
on
\mathcal{C}
, we can identify the sets
S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}\simeq[\bar{A}(x)]
with subsets of the set

S_{\bar{A}}=\{0,1,\ldots,\max_{x\in\mathcal{C}}\bar{A}(x)-1\}

(19)

via

n\mapsto\beta_{xy}(n)+\bar{A}(y).

The set
S_{\bar{A}}
will be helpful later when organizing certain framing data and reasononing about operations which change the stratification on
\mathcal{C}
.

Note that the set

S_{\bar{A}}^{-}=\{0,1,\ldots,\min_{x\in\mathcal{C}}\bar{A}(x)\}

(20)

is nonempty whenever
\bar{A}
never takes on the value
-1
; this set preciely the complement of all the images of all of the
S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}
.

Remark 24
.

When
\mathcal{C}
is equipped with an integral action, for the general discussion regarding operations on flow categories and Floer homotopy types, there is no loss in identifying elements of
S_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)
with their images under
\beta_{xy}
; we will do so freely in the discussion.

Definition 18
.

The objects of
\mathcal{C}
form the objects of a poset, with the partial order defined by the property that
x>y
if
\mathcal{C}(x,y)\neq\emptyset
,
x\neq y
. We will say that a pair
(x,y)
,
x>y
, is
primitive
if there are no objects
z
such that
x>y>z
; in other words,
(x,y)
is primitive if
G(x,y)=\emptyset
, where

G(x,y)=\left\{(x_{0},x_{1})\in\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}\,|\,\mathcal{C}(x,x_{0})\neq\emptyset,\mathcal{C}(x_{0},x_{1})\neq\emptyset,\mathcal{C}(x_{1},y)\neq\emptyset\right\}\setminus\{(x,y)\}.

(21)

A
primitive path
from
x
to
y
is a sequence of elements
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{r}=y
such that
x_{i}>x_{i+1}
for
i=1,\ldots,r
, and each pair
(x_{i},x_{i+1})
is primitive.

Definition 19
.

The category
Man^{\prime}
is the category with objects
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds, and morphims given by morphisms
(f,\bar{f})
of the corresponding objects in
\langle k\rangle-Spc^{\prime}
such that
\bar{f}
is a smooth map of manifolds with corners.

Definition 20
.

A
smooth flow category
is a pair
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
, where
\mathcal{C}
is a flow category with a grading
\mu
and an integral action
\bar{A}
, together with a category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
enriched in
Man^{\prime}
which gives
\mathcal{C}
upon applying the forgetful functor from
Man^{\prime}
to
\langle k\rangle-Spc^{\prime}
. Moreover, we must have that

\dim\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x,y)=\mu(x,y)

for all distinct pairs of objects
(x,y)
of
\mathcal{C}
for which
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x,y)
is nonempty.

As in differential topology, when the data of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is clear from the content, we will denote a smooth flow category
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
simply by
\mathcal{C}
, and think of
\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
as smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds.

Remark 25
.

This definition of a smooth flow category is essentially equivalent to the definition given in Cohen
[
29
]
when the integral action is
equal to
the grading. The decoupling of the stratification from the grading in the virtual setting is a necessary complication forced by the geometry.

Remark 26
.

We will now define a category of derived
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds and morphisms given by isomorphisms onto boundary strata of the codomain, in order to make sense of a derived analog of a smooth flow category. It is straightforward to work with topological or smooth analogs of such a definition. However, most naive constructions of compatible families of charts for moduli spaces in Floer homology do now have the property that that given
x>y>z
objects of a flow category with
T(x_{0},x_{1})
denoting the thickening of the moduli space of floer tracjectories
\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x,y)
, that

\partial T(x,z)=\cup_{x>y>z}\text{ in }T(x,y)\times T(y,z).

Instead, one has that boundary strata of
T(x,z)
are unions of
vector bundles
over the products
T(x,y)\times T(y,z)
, and more generally that the boundaries of the derived
\langle k\rangle
-manifold charts are
stabilizations
of the product charts associated to the boundary strata. The reason for this is because unless one works carefully, one needs
more
potential perturbations of the Floer equation to make the linearized operators all Floer trajectories in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x,z)
surjective than one gets from the choices previously made for the boundary strata.

However, in Section
7
, where we construct a virtual smoothing of the flow category of Hamiltonian Floer trajectories, we in particular explain how to carefully choose perturbation data so as to avoid these “stabilizing vector bundles” that show up in previous constructions
[
20
,
85
]
. It is possible to develop a version of a theory of flow categories where such stabilizing vector bundles are
allowed
; however, constructing and manipulating framings, embeddings and extensions of embeddings for such categories (see Definition
23
and below) is significantly more difficult, and we leave the study of such more general virtually smooth flow categories to future work.

Definition 21
.

The category
DerMan^{\prime}_{sm,no-stab}
is the category with objects given by derived
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds, and morphisms from the derived
\langle S_{\mathcal{T}}\rangle
-manifold
\mathcal{T}
to the derived
\langle S_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\rangle
-manifold
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=(T^{\prime},V^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
to be tuples
\mathfrak{f}=(f,\widetilde{f})=(f,(\bar{f},\bar{f}^{\prime}))
, where

•

f:S_{\mathcal{T}}\to S_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
is an injective map;

•

\bar{f}:T\to T^{\prime}
is a diffeomorphism onto its image
T^{\prime}(f(S_{\mathcal{T}}))
, such that
\bar{f}
induces diffeomorphisms
T(S^{\prime})\to T^{\prime}(f(S^{\prime}))
for each
S^{\prime}\subset S_{\mathcal{T}}
;

•

\bar{f}^{\prime}:V\to V^{\prime}|_{T^{\prime}(f(S_{\mathcal{T}}))}
is a bundle isomorphism covering
\bar{f}
, such that

\bar{f}^{\prime}\circ\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}\circ\bar{f}.

We define
DerMan^{\prime}_{top,no-stab}
to be the same category where
\mathcal{T}
is a topological derived
\langle k\rangle
-manifold and
\bar{f}
is only a homeomorphism. Similarly,
DerSp^{\prime}
is the same category where
\mathcal{T}
is only a derived
\langle k\rangle
-space and
\bar{f}
a homeomorphism.

Definition 22
.

A
virtually smooth flow category
(without stabilizations) is a pair
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
, where
\mathcal{C}
is a flow category with grading
\mu
and integral action
\bar{A}
, and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is a category enriched in
DerMan^{\prime}_{sm,no-stab}
equipped with an isomorphism to
\mathcal{C}
upon applying the forgetful functor
DerMan^{\prime}_{sm,no-stab}\to\langle k\rangle-Spc^{\prime}
, satisfying two further conditions:

•

Compatibility with the grading:

\vdim\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x,y)=\mu(x,y)

for all distinct pairs of objects
(x,y)
of
\mathcal{C}
;

•

Writing
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x,y)=(T_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)},V_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)},\sigma_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)})
, any element of
\partial T_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}
is in the image of the maps
\bar{f}
(Definition
21
) associated to some composition of morphism objects in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.

A
virtually topologically-smooth flow category
is the same definition with
DerMan^{\prime}_{sm,no-stab}
replaced with
DerMan^{\prime}_{top,no-stab}
. A
virtual topological flow category
is the same definition but with
DerMan^{\prime}_{top,no-stab}
replaced with
DerSp^{\prime}
.

Remark 27
.

Virtually (topologically-)smooth flow categories together with extra data be used to construct stable homotopy types; in this paper we will explain the construction for virtually smoooth flow categories (equipped with extra data), leaving the topologically smooth case to later work. In contrast, virtual topological flow categories do not carry homotopical meaning, but are simply a convenient method for packaging data produced in the process of producing virtually smooth flow categories (as in the proof of Proposition
21
).

4.4
Framings of flow categories

As discussed in Section
2
, any derived
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
\mathcal{T}=(T,V,\sigma)
carries a class in
KO^{0}(T)
given by the virtual vector bundle
[TT]-[V]
. Moreover, using index theory
8.6
one may be able to arrange for this class to equal to a class of the form
[\mathbb{R}^{\vdim\mathcal{T}}]
, or in other words (as in Section
2
) for there to be some vector bundle
W
such that the stabilization
\mathcal{T}_{W}=(T_{W},V_{W},\sigma_{W})
of
\mathcal{T}
by
W
has both
TT_{W}\simeq\underline{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
and
V_{W}\simeq\underline{\mathbb{R}}^{N-\vdim\mathcal{T}}
for some
N
. In this section, we spell out precisely precisely is a compatible family of such pairs of isomorphisms across all morphisms in a flow category. First however we define some notation that is helpful for discussing framings for
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds.

Tangent bundles of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds.

We now recall some basic structures associated to the tangent bundles of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds. The model
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
, have trivial tangent bundles
T(\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell})
which, when restricted to
(\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell})(S)=\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}(S)\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
, contain the trivial subbundles
T(\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell})(S)
. The perpendiculars to these sub-bundles are canonically trivial: for each
j\in[k]\setminus S
, there is a vector in
T(\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell})(S)^{\perp}
that is the
inwards-pointing normal vector
to
(\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell})_{j}
(in the notation of Definition
7
).

Similar structures exist on the tangent bundles of a general
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
X
, namely, subbundles
TX(S)
of
TX|_{X(S)}
. By choosing a convenient of Riemannian metric on
X
(Definition
49
), one can show that on any
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
X
, there exist decompositions
TX|_{X(S)}=TX(S)\oplus\underline{\mathbb{R}}^{[k]\setminus S}
which satisfy natural compatibility conditions between the strata; choosing a convenient Riemannian metric on
X
will determine these decompositions uniquely.

Given a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
X
, write
\underline{T\mathbb{R}}^{k}_{+}
to be the trivial
\mathbb{R}^{k}
bundle over
X
, together with the distinguished subundles
\underline{\mathbb{R}}^{[k]\setminus S}\subset\underline{T\mathbb{R}}^{k}_{+}|_{X(S)}
for all
S\subset[k]
. The natural notion of a trivialization of the tangent bundle
TX
of
X
is a vector bundle isomorphism
\eta:TX\to\underline{T\mathbb{R}}^{k}_{+}\times\underline{\mathbb{R}}^{\dim X-k}
which induces isomorphisms
TX(S)\to\underline{T\mathbb{R}}^{k}_{+}(S)\times\underline{\mathbb{R}}^{\dim X-k}
for all
S\subset[k]
. Of course, this notion immediately generalizes to
\langle S\rangle
-manifolds for general finite sets
S
.

How to frame a flow category.

We now define a framing of a virtually smooth flow category:

Definition 23
.

A
framing
of a virtually smooth flow category without stabilizations
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
is a sequence, for all pairs
x,y\in\mathcal{C}
with
x>y
, of vector bundle isomorphisms

\eta_{xy}:TT(x,y)\to\underline{T\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x,y)}\times\underline{V^{0}(x,y)}

\eta^{\prime}_{xy}:V(x,y)\to\underline{V^{1}(x,y)}

Here the vector spaces
V^{0}(x,y)
,
V^{1}(x,y)
, and
W^{0}(x,y,z)
are choices of finite-dimensional vector spaces that are part of the data of the framing. The remaining data of a framing are isomorphisms of inner product spaces

\begin{gathered}\tau^{0}_{xyz}:V^{0}(x,y)\oplus V^{0}(y,z)\to V^{0}(x,z),\\
\tau^{1}_{xyz}:V^{1}(x,y)\oplus V^{1}(y,z)\to V^{1}(x,z).\end{gathered}

(22)

Moreover, the maps
\{(\eta_{xy}),(\eta^{\prime}_{xy}),(\eta_{xyz}),(\tau^{0}_{xyz}),(\tau^{1}_{xyz})\}
are required to satisfy the following compatibility conditions.

•

The isomorphism
\eta_{xy}
are trivializations of
TT(x,y)
as the tangent bundle of a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold; thus, using the isomorphisms
S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}\simeq[\bar{A}(x)-\bar{A}(y)-1]
of Definition
16
, we have that
\eta_{xy}
induces isomorphisms

TT(x,y)(S)\to\underline{T\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)-\bar{A}(y)-1}(S^{\prime})\times\underline{V^{0}(x,y)}

for all
S^{\prime}\subset S
;

•

For all triples of objects
(x,y,z)
of
\mathcal{C}
,
we require that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT(x,y)\times TT(y,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT(x,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x,y)}}\oplus\underline{V^{0}(x,y)}\times\underline{\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(y,z)}}\oplus\underline{V^{0}(y,z)}\times\underline{W^{0}(x,y,z)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x,z)}}\times\underline{V^{0}(x,z)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d\bar{f}_{xyz}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{xy}\boxplus\eta_{yz}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{xz}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}

(23)

where the bottom map
r
is the bundle map covering
\bar{f}_{xyz}
induced from the composition of the reordering and inclusion map

\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x,y)}\oplus V^{0}(x,y)\oplus\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(y,z)}\to\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x,y)}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(y,z)}\oplus V^{0}(x,y)\subset\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x,z)}\times V^{0}(x,y),

with
\tau_{xyz}^{0}
(
22
).

•

Finally, continuing with the notation of the previous item, the diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(x,y)\boxplus V(y,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(x,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{V^{1}(x,y)}\oplus\underline{V^{1}(y,z)}\oplus}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{V^{1}(x,z)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{f}^{\prime}_{xyz}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\prime}_{xy}\boxplus\eta^{\prime}_{yz})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\prime}_{xz}}

(24)

where the bottom horizontal arrow is the map of trivial bundles covering
\bar{f}_{xyz}
induced from
\tau^{1}_{xyz}
(
22
).

Parameterizations of framings.

The data of the maps
\{(\tau^{0}_{xyz}),(\tau^{1}_{xyz})\}
associated to a framing of a flow category is essentially ‘homotopically auxiliary’. We will not make this statement completely precise in this paper. We will refer to the data
(\{V^{0}(x,y)\},\{V^{1}(x,y)\},\{(\tau^{0}_{xyz}),(\tau^{1}_{xyz})\})
as the
parameterization
of the framing; specifically, it is an
F^{\prime}_{2}
-parameterization (Lemma
8
). The data of the framing forces the existence of many compatibility conditions between the maps
\{(\tau^{0}_{xyz}),(\tau^{1}_{xyz})\}
, which are spelled out in this lemma. We discuss these data and the relevant terminology further in Section
5.2
.

Definition 24
.

A framed flow category is
semi-freely parameterized
when the associated
F^{\prime}_{2}
-parameterization is an
F^{s}_{2}
-parameterization. This notions and related notions are defined in Section
5.2
.

Canonical parameterizations of framings.

Now, there are canonical maps

\begin{gathered}r_{a,a^{\prime},b,b^{\prime}}:\mathbb{R}^{a}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{a^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{b}\times\mathbb{R}^{b^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{R}^{a+b+1}\times\mathbb{R}^{a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}}\\
r_{a,a^{\prime},b,b^{\prime}}(x,y,z,t)=((x,0,z),(y,t)).\end{gathered}

(25)

Definition 25
.

We say that a framing is
canonically parameterized
if all vector spaces
V^{0}(x,y),V^{1}(x,y)
are simply euclidean vector spaces
\mathbb{R}^{n}
of the appropriate dimension and the maps in the bottom of the diagrams (
23
), (
24
) are induced from canonical isomorphisms (
25
).

Remark 28
.

In previous work on flow categories
[
27
,
71
]
, one considers framed flow categories without stabilizations for which the framings are canonically parameterized. An
F_{2}^{s}
parameterization is a generalization of a canonical parameterization which allows for a direct sum of a canonical parameterization with a a ’free parameterization’ (an
F_{2}
-parameterization).

We are forced to introduce more general parameterizations of framings in our definition of a framing of a virtually smooth flow category in order to deal with framings of
G
-equivariant flow-categories. Specifically, there is an analog of a canonically-paramerized
G
-equivariant flow category, but unfortunately, when one views this flow category as an
H
-equivariant flow category for
H\subset G
, its framing will no longer be canonically parameterized. For further discussion on the kinds of parameterizations of framings that we use, see Section
5.2
.

Embeddings of framings.

Definition 26
.

An
embedding
of a framing of a virtually smooth flow category is a sequence of finite-dimensional inner product spaces
V^{0}(x),V^{1}(x)
, such that
together with isometric embeddings

\bar{\alpha}^{0}_{xy}:V^{0}(x,y)\oplus V^{0}(y)\to V^{0}(x),\bar{\alpha}^{1}_{xy}:V^{1}(x,y)\oplus V^{1}(y)\to V^{1}(x)

such that the following diagram commutes for
i=0,1
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{i}(x,y)\oplus V^{i}(y,z)\oplus V^{0}(z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{i}(x,y)\oplus V^{i}(y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{i}(x,z)\oplus V^{i}(z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{i}(x).}

(26)

A framing with a chosen embedding is said to be embedded. We write
\alpha^{i}_{xy}:V^{i}(x,y)\to V^{i}(x)

,i=0,1
, for the restriction of
\bar{\alpha}^{i}_{xy}
, and
\hat{\alpha}^{i}_{xy}
for the restriction of
\bar{\alpha}^{i}_{xy}
to
V^{i}(y)
.

We will refer to each of the data
(\{V^{i}(x)\},\{\hat{\alpha}^{i}_{xy}\})
for
i=0,1
as
E^{\prime}
-parameterizations
, and the union of these data for
i=0
and
i=1
as an
E^{\prime}_{2}
-parameterization. An embedding of a framing the same as the data of an
E^{\prime}_{2}
-parameterization together with an isomorphism of the associated
F^{\prime}_{2}
-parameterization with the one underlying the framing. We discuss these notions further in
5.2
.

Lemma 1
.

Semi-freely parameterized framings of virtually smooth flow categories can be embedded.

Proof.

This is a tautology by Definitions
60
,
64
, and
65
.
∎

Definition 27
.

An embedded framing of a graded virtually smooth flow category
\mathcal{C}
is
adapted
, or
compatible with the grading
, if

\dim V^{0}(x)-\dim V^{1}(x)=\mu(x)-(\bar{A}(x)+1).

(27)

for all
x\in\mathcal{C}
.

Remark 29
.

Implicitly, we can use the formula above to
define

\mu
given a framed virtually-smooth flow category with an embedded framing; with this choice of
\mu
the category
\mathcal{C}
would be adapted. From this perspective the grading is actually auxiliary data, and the embedding of the framing is a geometric enrichment of the concept of a grading.

4.5
Auxiliary data for flow categories.

Definition 28
.

A flow category
\mathcal{C}
is
hom-proper
if the morphism spaces
\mathcal{C}(x,y)
are compact for all pairs of objects
x,y\in\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 29
.

A flow category
\mathcal{C}
is integral action
\bar{A}
is proper if it is hom-proper and the sets

\{x\in\mathcal{C}|\bar{A}\leq C\}

are finite for all real numbers
C
.

Definition 30
.

A flow category
\mathcal{C}
is
finite
if it is hom-proper and has a finite number of objects.

Embeddings of flow categories.

Definition 31
.

An
embedding

\mathbf{E}
of a finite virtually smooth flow category
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
with an embedded framing
(\eta_{x,y},\eta^{\prime}_{x,y})
is a sequence of proper codimension-0 embeddings (the
embedding maps
)

\iota_{xy}:T(x,y)\to\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x,y)}\times V^{0}(x,y).

(28)

such that the following conditions are satsified. First, the following diagrams commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(x,y)\times T(y,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x,y)}\times V^{0}(x,y)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(y,z)}\times V^{0}(y,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(x,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x,z)}\times V^{0}(x,z)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{f}_{xyz}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{xy}\times\iota_{yz}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{xz}}

(29)

where
r
is the composition of a reordering map and the map
\tau^{0}_{xyz}
of (
22
), as in (
23
). Second, one has that for each pair of objects
x>y
of
\mathcal{C}
, one has that

d\iota_{xy}|_{TT(x,t)_{j}^{\perp}}
maps to the inwards pointing normal vector of
(\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x,y)}_{+})_{j}
.

Finally, we require that for each
x\in\mathcal{C}
and each
a<\bar{A}(x)
, writing

\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{r}\}=\{y\in\mathcal{C}:x>y,\bar{A}(y)=a\}

we have that the images of

T(x,y_{i})\times V^{0}(y_{i})\text{ in }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x,y)}\times V^{0}(x)\text{ under }(id\times\bar{\alpha}_{xy_{i}})\circ(\iota_{xy_{i}}\times id)

are disjoint as
i
ranges over
1,\ldots,r
.

One-point compactifications and wedge products.

If
X
is a non-compact space without basepoint, its one-point compactification
X_{*}
is compact space with a basepoint at infinity. One immediately verifies that for a pair of spaces
(X,Y)
without basepoint, one has a canonical identification

(X\times Y)_{*}=X_{*}\wedge Y_{*}.

(30)

(where if
X
is compact we use
X_{*}
to denote the addition of a disjoint basepoint
*
).

The one-point compactifications of Euclidean spaces are all spheres with a base-point, i.e.

\mathbb{R}^{n}_{*}=S^{n},

(31)

and one has the canonical identification

(\mathbb{R}^{1}_{+}\times X)_{*}=c(X_{*})=\{[0,1]\times X\}/((0,x)\sim(t,*)\text{ for any pair}(t,x)\in[0,1]\times X);

(32)

here
c(X)
is the
cone
on the pointed space
X_{*}
. Thus, we think of
(\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell})_{*}
as the
iterated cone
on the sphere
S^{\ell}
; these spaces arise in the Cohen-Jones-Segal construction
[
27
]
.

Extensions of embeddings of flow categories

Definition 32
.

Let us assume now that we have an embedding
(\iota_{xy})
of a finite virtually smooth flow category
\mathcal{C}
an embedded framing. An
extension

\bar{\mathbf{E}}
of the embedding
\mathbf{E}
is the data of
extension maps

e_{xy}:(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x,y)}\times V^{0}(x,y))_{*}\to(V^{1}(x,y))_{*}

(33)

These maps are required to satisfy five conditions:

(A)

There exists an open subset
U_{xy}\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x,y)}\times V^{0}(x,y)
such that

U_{xy}\supset\overline{V_{xy}}=\overline{Im\iota_{xy}}

which moreover deformation retracts to
U_{xy}
, and such
e_{xy}|_{U_{xy}^{c}}
is the constant map to the basepoint;

(B)

For each
x
and each
A_{r}<\bar{A}(x)
, we have that the aforementioned sets

\{\overline{U_{xy}}_{x>y,\bar{A}(y)=A_{r}}

are pairwise disjoint;

(C)

On
V_{xy}
we have that
e_{xy}=\check{\eta}^{\prime}_{xy}\sigma_{xy}\circ\iota_{xy}^{-1}
; here
\check{\eta^{\prime}}_{xy}
is the composition of the framing
\eta^{\prime}_{xy}
of the obstruction bundle with the projection to the fiber
V^{1}(x,y)
.

(D)

The maps
e_{xy}
are compatible with composition. This means, first, that for every
x>y>z
in
\mathcal{C}
we have that the image of
U_{xy}\times U_{yz}
under the map

\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x,y)}\times V^{0}(x,y)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(y,z)}\times V^{0}(y,z)\simeq\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x,y)}\times\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(y,z)}\times V^{0}(x,y)\times V^{0}(y,z)\xrightarrow{i\times\tau^{0}_{xyz}}\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x,z)}\times V^{0}(x,z)

lies inside
U_{xz}
. Second, we also have that, viewing
U_{xy}\times U_{yz}
under the inclusion above, we have that
e_{xz}|_{U_{xy}\times U_{yz}}=\tau^{1}_{xyz}(e_{xy}\times e_{yz})_{*}
, where
\tau^{1}_{xyz}(\cdot)_{*}
denotes the homeomorphism

\tau^{1}_{xyz}(\cdot)_{*}:V^{1}(x,y)_{*}\vee V^{1}(y,z)_{*}\to V^{1}(x,z)_{*}

induced by monoidal property of the
1
-point-compactification functor.

(E)

The preimage of
0
under
e_{xy}
is exactly
\iota_{xy}(\sigma^{-1}_{xy}(0))
. Finally,

(F)

Recall that our conditions on virtually smooth flow categories require that if
\sigma^{-1}_{xy}(0)\cap T(x,y)(S)=\emptyset
then
T(x,y)(S)=\emptyset
. We require that if
T(x,y)(S)=\emptyset
then
U_{xy}\cap\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x,y)}_{+}(S)\times V^{0}(x,y)=\emptyset
; thus,
e_{xy}
sends strata not containing any points sent to zero by
\sigma_{xy}

entirely
to the basepoint.

4.6
The Virtual CJS construction.

Suppose we are given an adapted freely framed virtually smooth flow category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
with an extension of of an embedding, as in Definition
32
.

Set
V^{1}_{max}
to be the shift space associated to the parameterization
\{V(x)\}
of the framing of the obstruction bundles of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
(Definition
63
); thus
V^{1}(x)
is canonically identified as a subspace of
V^{1}_{max}
, and there is a canonical orthogonal complement
(V^{1}(z))^{\perp}
. In particular, we have canonical isomorphisms

V^{1}(x,z)\oplus V^{1}(x)^{\perp}\simeq V^{1}(z)^{\perp}

(34)

for any
x>z
in
\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 33
.

The stable homotopy type associated to an extension of an embedding of a virtually smooth flow category
\mathcal{C}
is given by

\begin{gathered}|\mathcal{C}|=\Sigma^{-(V^{1}_{max})}\Sigma^{\infty}\{\mathcal{C}\},\text{ where }\\
\{\mathcal{C}\}=\left(\bigvee_{z\in\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x)\oplus V^{1}(x)^{\perp})_{*}/\sim\right).\end{gathered}

(35)

The relations are defined below. Each term
(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x)\times V^{1}(x)^{\perp})_{*}
is homeomorphic to a disk with base-point on the boundary; the relations be given by inductively attaching these disks to one another along a diagram organized by the poset
\mathcal{C}
. More explicitly, writing
\{\mathcal{C}\}_{\mathcal{P}}\subset\{\mathcal{C}\}
for the subset which is the image of the part of the wedge product under the quotient by the relations of all
z\in\mathcal{P}
, then if
\mathcal{P}
is a downwards closed subposet of
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{P}_{x}=\mathcal{P}\cup x
where there is no
y\in\mathcal{C}\setminus\mathcal{P}
such that
x>y>z
with
z\in\mathcal{P}
, then

\{\mathcal{C}\}_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\text{ is the cone on }\tilde{e}_{x}:\partial(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x)\times V^{1}(x)^{\perp})_{*}\to\{\mathcal{C}\}_{\mathcal{P}}.

(36)

To define the relations we will define these maps. First off, the relations for
\mathcal{P}=M(\mathcal{C})
collapse the boundaries of the corresponding terms in the wedge product; in other words, if
\bar{A}(z)>-1
, we identify
\partial(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x)\oplus V^{1}(x)^{\perp})_{*}\subset(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x)\oplus V^{1}(x)^{\perp})_{*}
with the basepoint. (In other words, we set the maps
\tilde{e}_{z}
to be the maps to the basepoint for all
z\in M(\mathcal{C})
, whenever the domain of
\tilde{e}_{z}
is nonempty, i.e. when
\bar{A}(z)>-1
.
Thus

\{\mathcal{C}\}_{M(\mathcal{C})}=\bigvee_{z\in M(\mathcal{C})}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x)\oplus V^{1}(x)^{\perp})_{*}/\partial.

Now, note that
\partial(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x)\times V^{1}(x)^{\perp})_{*}
can be divided into
\bar{A}(x)+1
pieces
\partial_{i}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x)\times V^{1}(x)^{\perp})_{*}
for
i=1,\ldots,\bar{A}(x)+1
, with the
i
-th piece given by the subset of the boundary where the
i
-th coordinate of the
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}
factor is set to zero. We have a canonical homeomorphism

\partial_{i}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x))_{*}\simeq(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{i-1}\times V^{0}(x)\times V^{1}(x)^{\perp})\wedge(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1-i})_{*}.

We will define maps

\tilde{e}_{x,i}:\partial_{i}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x)\times V^{1}(x)^{\perp})_{*}\to\{\mathcal{C}\}_{\mathcal{P}_{x>}}

where we write
\mathcal{P}_{x>}
to be the set of elements of
\mathcal{C}
which are strictly less than
x
. The map
\tilde{e}_{x}
is defined such that

\tilde{e}_{x,i}=\tilde{e}_{x}|_{\partial_{i}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x))_{*}};

this inductive definition of the attaching maps allows us to define the full Floer homotopy type by induction along
\mathcal{C}
.

Let us now define
\tilde{e}_{x,i}
.,
fixing
i\in 1,\ldots,\bar{A}(x)+1
. Essentially,
\tilde{e}_{x,i}
is the unique map we can define using the data of the
\tilde{e}_{x,y}
as
y
ranges over
\{y\in\mathcal{P}_{x}:\bar{A}(y)=i\}
, by “superimposing” these maps on one another. For each such
y
, we set

U^{\prime}_{xy}=\bar{\alpha}_{xy}^{0}(U_{xy}\times V^{0}(y)),U^{\prime\prime}_{xy}=U^{\prime}_{xy}\times(V^{1}(x)^{\perp}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1-i})_{*}.

In the complement of all
U^{\prime\prime}_{xy}
, we set
\tilde{e}_{x,i}
to map to the basepoint; and in each
U^{\prime\prime}_{xy}
we set
\tilde{e}_{x,i}
to be the composition

\begin{gathered}U^{\prime}_{xy}\times(V^{1}(x)^{\perp}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1-i})_{*}\xrightarrow{(e_{xy}\wedge id)\circ(\bar{\alpha}^{0}_{xy})^{-1}\wedge id}(V^{1}(x,y)\times V^{0}(y)\times V^{1}(x)^{\perp}\times(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1-i})_{*}\simeq\\
\simeq(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1-i}\times V^{0}(y)\times V^{1}(y)^{\perp})_{*}\to\{\mathcal{C}\}_{\mathcal{P}_{x>}}.\end{gathered}

(37)

Here the isomorphism from the right hand side of the first line to the left hand side of the second line is just rearranging factors and using the isomorphism (
34
), as well as the fact that
\bar{A}(y)=\bar{A}-i
so the dimensions of the
\mathbb{R}_{+}
factors agree. The left hand side of the bottom line is manifestly one of the terms of the wedge product in (
35
), and so the bottom map is the inclusion into the quotient by the previously defined relations. Notice the map
\tilde{e}_{x,i}
is the composition of of a manifestly well-defined map

\tilde{e}^{\prime}_{x,i}:\partial_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x)\times V^{1}(x)^{\perp})_{*}\to\bigvee_{y\in\mathcal{P}_{x>}:\bar{A}(y)=\bar{A}(x)-i}(\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(y)+1}\times V^{0}(y)\times V^{1}(y)^{\perp})_{*}

sending the boundary the domain to the boundary of the codomain, with the inclusion of the codomain of
\tilde{e}^{\prime}_{x,i}
into
\{\mathcal{C}\}_{\mathcal{P}_{x>}}
.

Lemma 2
.

The attatching maps
\tilde{e}_{x}
defined above are well-defined.

Proof.

(Sketch.)
The non-overlap condition in the definition of an extension of an embedding means that the maps
\tilde{e}_{x,i}
are each well defined. So the only question is whether these definitions agree on the boundaries; let’s consider whether they agree on the intersection
\partial_{i}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x)\times V^{1}(x)^{\perp})_{*}
and
\partial_{j}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x)\times V^{1}(x)^{\perp})_{*}
, for
i\neq j
. For notation let us call these strata
\partial_{i}C
and
\partial_{j}C
. Without loss of generality suppose
i<j
. Let
U_{x,y_{1}},\ldots,U_{x,y_{r}}
be the open sets arising when defining
\tilde{e}_{x,j}
and similarly let
U_{x,w_{t}}
be the open sets arising when defining
\tilde{e}_{x,i}
. the compatibility condition for the extension of the embedding tells us that when the associated open sets
U^{\prime\prime}_{x,y_{r}}\subset\partial_{j}C
, when restricted to
\partial_{i}C\cap\partial_{j}C
, become images of sets of the form

U_{x,z_{\ell r}}\times U_{z_{\ell r},y_{r}}\times V^{0}(y_{r})\times(\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x)+i-j}_{+})_{*}

where
\bar{A}(z_{\ell r})=\bar{A}(U_{w_{t}})=c
for some
c
independent of
\ell,r,t
.
Dropping the point at infinity, we see that the disjointness condition for extensions of embeddings embeddings but now applied to the
U_{x,z_{\ell r}}\times V^{0}(z_{\ell r})\subset\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x,c)}_{+}\times V^{0}(x)
as well as the
U_{x,w_{t}}\times V^{0}(w_{t})\subset\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x,c)}\times V_{0}(x)
implies that two of these sets overlap if and only if
z_{\ell r}=w_{t}
for some
\ell,r,t
. But in that case, we see that for any point
\tilde{u}
in
U^{\prime\prime}_{x,w_{t}}\cap\partial_{j}C
,
e_{i,x}(\tilde{u})
will be identified with a point which is the image of the boundary sphere of the wedge factor associated to
w_{t}=z_{\ell r}
in
\{\mathcal{C}\}_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}
. The condition that the extension of the emebdding is compatible with composition then implies that
\tilde{e}_{j,x}(\tilde{u})
will be sent to the same point in
\{\mathcal{C}\}_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}
.
∎

Thus, an extension of an embedding of a framed virtually smooth flow category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
defines a canonical associated space
\{\mathcal{C}\}
(implicitly depending on the framing, embedding, and extension) and a corresponding spectrum
|\mathcal{C}|
. We will see in the subsequent Section
4.7
that it is elementary to extend all these definitions to the equivariant setting. The space
\{\mathcal{C}\}
is built out of disks one for each object of
\mathcal{C}
, attached inductively along the poset
\mathcal{C}
with attaching maps determined by the chosen embeddings of the thickenings. In the
G
-equivariant setting, these disks become compactifications of
G
-representations and the attaching maps (and the poset
\mathcal{C}
itself) are all equivariant as well. The desuspension in the definition of
|\mathcal{C}|
is chosen such the spectrum
|\mathcal{C}|
is filtered by subspectra with associated graded given by stable spheres, with the stable sphere corresponding to
x\in\mathcal{C}
being of the form
\mathbb{S}^{\mu(x)}
where
\mu
is the grading on
\mathcal{C}
(Section
10.2
). Thus,
\{\mathcal{C}\}
has the homotopy type of a CW complex, and
|\mathcal{C}|
defines an object in the Spanier-Whitehead category (Section
3
). It is a stable homotopy analog of a finite
CW
complex, and thus a stable homotopy refinement of the finite-dimensional chain complex produced by Floer homology. In the equivariant setting, the stable spheres
\mathbb{S}^{\mu(x)}
are replaced by equivariant stable spheres
\mathbb{S}^{[\mu](x)}
, where
[\mu](x)
is a virtual
G
-representation. In the Floer theoretic situation, this virtual
G
-representation should be thought of as the stable equivariant spheres corresponding to the virtual representation given by the difference between the positive and the negative eigenvalues of the Floer action functional, (=or, more rigorously, the equivariant spectral flow of a path from the self-adjoint operator given by Hessian at a given critical point to a base-point self-adjoint operator. The latter notion is made completely formal in Section
8
and and specialized to the discussion of Hamiltonian Floer Homology in Section
8.6
.

Thus, after the discussion in Section
4.7
of the equivariant situation, we will know how to produce a genuine equivariant stable homotopy type from an equivariant analog of the above data. The following Figures
4
and
5
explain what the equivariant Floer homotopy type looks like in simple examples; the general case is essentially an amalgamation of the cases described in those figures. The subsequent Section
5
explains how to actually
produce
framings and embeddings of framings of a framed virtually smooth (equivariant) flow category; and the actual virtually smooth flow categories associated to Hamiltonian Floer homology are produced in Section
7
and framed in Section
8
. Using this data one can produce the equivariant stable homotopy types associated to Hamiltonian Floer Homology; this is done in Section
10
. Similarly, geometric constructions described in these aforementioned sections allow us to relate these flow categories using the continuation map equation and its homotopies; the pattern is quite general, and fairly general tools regarding the manipulation of homotopy types using flow categories are described in Section
10.2
.

In the following remarks, we comment on several aspects of the above construction.

Remark 30
.

The Floer homotopy type
|\mathcal{C}|
may seem to depend not just on the framed virtually smooth flow category, but on its embedding and the extension of its embedding. However, Section
5
below develops certain tools which allow one to relate the Floer homotopy type of
\mathcal{C}
to that of a
stabilization
of
\mathcal{C}
, or a
restratification
of
\mathcal{C}
. Neither of these turn out to change the (genuine equivariant) homotopy type up to canonical weak equivalence; moreover, while we do not write this out in this paper, the tools of Section
5
and an elementary parameterized homotopy theory argument can establish that
the floer homotopy type
\mathcal{C}
does not depend on the (extension of) the embedding of the framed virtual smoothing
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, but only on the (semi-freely) framed virtual smoothing
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
itself,
up to canonical weak equivariant homotopy equivalence. Thus, we should think of the framed virtually smooth category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
as representing a (possibly equivariant) stable homotopy type.

Remark 31
.

Analogously to Definition
12
and the discussion about the Pontrjagin-Thom construction in Section
2
, the subsequent Section
5
shows that the floer homotopy type
|\mathcal{C}^{\prime}|
actually only depends on the framed virtually smooth flow category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
up to stabilization. Similarly, the results of that section show that the data of the integral action
\bar{A}
is essentially auxiliary (and this will be used to show invariance of the equivariant floer homotopy type
|\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)|
in Section
10.4
).

Remark 32
.

One sees by observation that while the data of the framings of the obstruction bundle and all aspects of the parameterization of the framing of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
plays a role in the definition of the Floer homotopy type, the actual framings of the tangent bundles of the thickenings of the the morphism objects of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
play no role in the definition of the Floer homotopy type. However, the process of
producing
embeddings of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
(see Proposition
3
) involves heavy use of the Pontrjagin-Thom construction, which involves the framings of the thickenings. One can verify that if one changes the framing of the thickenings one can also thus affect the eventual Floer homotopy type produced, in a sense that can be seen in the following example. Given a virtually smooth flow category
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
with two objects
x
and
y
, the Floer homotopy type
|\mathcal{C}^{\prime}|
produced by applying Proposition
3
and the subsequent disscussion to produce the required extension of an embedding, will manifestly agree with the vitual Pontrjagin Thom construction on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x,y)
, performed as described in in the introdution. Twisting the framing of the thickening of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x,y)
will thus, in general, change the Floer homotopy type.

Remark 33
.

Because the Floer homotopy type is really an invariant of the framed virtual smoothing
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x,y)
up to stabilization, one should think of the
space
of framings as colimit of the spaces of framings of all the virtually smooth flow categories
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x,y)_{\mathcal{E}}
produced by stabilizing
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x,y)
by an arbitrarily large semi-free parameterization
\mathcal{E}
. This space is highly nontrivial; in the case of a flow category with
2
objects and with
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x,y)
simply a Kuranishi chart on a point with no obstruction bundle, this space can be identified with
O=\colim_{n}O(n)
, (and in the case of a
G
-action with its equivariant analog described in Appendix
11.1
). The fact that this space is nontrivial even in the case of such a trivial flow category has to do with
parameterized Floer theory
: one can imagine a
parameterized family of flow categories
with fiber isomorphic to the given one and the framings (after stabilization of the entire family) twisting as one changes the parameter in the family, giving rise to a parameterized homotopy type that is not the same as the produce of the usual homotopy type with the parameter space. We do not develop the parameterized theory of virtually smooth flow categories in this paper, and we leave this to further works.

Remark 34
.

The filtration on the homotopy space
\{\mathcal{C}\}
by the poset underlying
\mathcal{C}
is in general
not
a CW filtration, and the space is generally
not
a CW complex, but only a space built inductively by attaching cells. Such a phenomenon arises whenever one is studying the setting where there are flow lines at are
obstructed
, or
not regular
, as in the example of Figure
1
. The caption of Figure
5
explains how the corresponding Floer homotopy space looks like for that example. In contrast, in all previous constructions of a Floer homotopy type, the latter is built by initially building a space
which is a CW complex
with the CW filtration refining to a filtration by
\mathcal{C}
. The new phenomenon in our case arises because we stratify by the
auxiliary integral action

\bar{A}
rather than by the index
\mu
,
unlike all previous constructions
. This in turn is forced on us by the need to study obstructed Floer trajectories, which force the introduction of additional strata which are disallowed in the regular case.

Remark 35
.

An analogous situation to the previous remark arises very clearly in the setting of Morse theory. A Morse function (say, with distinct critical values) on a closed manifold
Y
defines a filtered space
Y^{\leq\bullet}
by the filtration by the sublevel sets of its critical values, even when it is not Morse-Smale. however, when the Morse function is Morse-Smale, the corresponding filtered space
is a CW complex

[
83
]
. As one moves between Morse functions through Morse-smale functions, the filtrations change continuously, moving through a non-CW filtration in between two different CW complex structures on
Y
. The phenomenon in the previous remark is an abstracted analog of this example; an essential insight of this paper is that
a non-regular floer action functional defines a well defined filtered stable homootopy type, even as it does not define a well-defined chain complex!

Figure 4:
Flow category with multiple outputs.
Given a flow category with three objects
x,y,z
, with nontrivial morphisms
x\to y
and
x\to z
, one produces the resulting homotopy type by finding disjoint (equivariant) embeddings of (stabilizations of)
T(x,y)
and
T(x,z)
into a vector space, and then extending the defining sections (which can be thought of as taking values in a wedge of spheres, each sphere coming from stabilizing
V(x,y)
or
V(x,z)
appropriately and compactifying) to the rest of the one-point compactification by requiring that on the complement of a slightly larger thickening of the images of
T(x,y)
and
T(x,z)
one maps to the basepoint of the corresponding wedge of spheres. If the maps in the stable homotopy category associated to
x\to y
and
x\to z
are
f_{xy}:\mathbb{S}^{V(x)}\to\mathbb{S}^{V(y)}
and
f_{xz}:\mathbb{S}^{V(x)}\to\mathbb{S}^{V(z)}
, respectively, then the resulting map represents the canonical map
(f_{xy}\vee 0)+(0\vee f_{yz}):\mathbb{S}^{V(x)}\to\mathbb{S}^{V(y)}\vee\mathbb{S}^{V(z)}.
. See Lemma
57
.

Figure 5:
Flow category with composable morphisms.
This figure depicts (on the left) the embedding of a flow category
\mathcal{C}
with post depicting as on the bottom right, with all arrows depicting regular isolated flow lines of a
C_{2}
-equivariant Morse function. the embedding of the thickening of
\mathcal{C}(x,y)
is sitting in
\mathbb{R}_{xy}
, and the embedding of the thickening of
\mathcal{C}(x,z)
is sitting in
\mathbb{R}_{yz}
, as depicted on the left. These embeddings determine the embedding of the boundary of the thickening of
\mathcal{C}(x,z)
(which is the product of the previously mentioned thickenings – the blue squares in the figure), which must be extended to the interior of
\mathbb{R}_{yz}\times\mathbb{R}_{xy}
(green). Note how the framing will be forced to twist along this embedding of the thickening of
\mathcal{C}(x,z)
, leading to the conclusion that
d^{2}=0
in the resulting Morse complex. In the final construction of the Floer homotopy type (Definition
33
), the cell associated to
x
is essentially the ‘cone’ on the figure on the right. Note that the embedding for the thickening of the virtual smoothing of Figure
3
looks very similar, except for two modifications. First, in order to embed
\mathcal{C}(y,z)
we must stabilize the previously zero-dimensional Kuranishi charts to
1
-dimensional Kuranishi charts by a copy of the sign representation (so that equivariant an embedding exists!); this makes making the obstruction bundle of
\mathcal{C}(x,z)
have fiber
\mathbb{R}_{sgn}^{2}
, and
\mathbb{R}_{yz}
has a nontrivial
C_{2}
action. The other difference is that
\mathbb{R}_{xy}
in this latter case is zero dimensional (so one slices the picture vertically at the point
u_{3}
). The extension of the obstruction section to
\mathbb{R}_{xy}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
(corresponding to the extension of the embedding) has no zeros on the interior, and upon perturbation will have an isolated finite set of zeros on the interior.The final Floer homotopy space is inductively built by attaching disk but the corresponding filtration does not me the space into a CW complex, as the attaching map from the boundary of the
3
-dimensional cell corresponding to
x
to the
3
-dimensional cell corresponding to
y
(here the extra dimension comes from
V(y)^{\perp}
in the formula for
\{\mathcal{C}\}
)is not the zero map (although it is zero in the homotopy category).

4.7
Equivariant Flow Categories

In this section, we generalize the earlier constructions of Section
4
to the setting to the equivariant setting.

Definition 34
.

Let
G
be a finite group. A
G
-equivariant flow category
\mathcal{C}
is a flow category
\mathcal{C}
together with an action of
G
on
Ob(\mathcal{C})
, and for each
g\in G
and each pair
(x,y)
of objects of
\mathcal{C}
such that
x>y
, an isomorphism in
\langle k\rangle
-Spc’

i^{g}_{x,y}=(\hat{i}^{g}_{x,y}:S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}\to S_{\mathcal{C}(gx,gy)},\bar{i}^{g}_{x,y}:\mathcal{C}(x,y)\to\mathcal{C}(gx,gy))

g_{xy}:\mathcal{C}(x,y)\to\mathcal{C}(gx,gy)

satisfying

•

g^{\prime}_{gx,gy}g_{x,y}=(g^{\prime}g)_{x,y}

for all

x>y
in
\mathcal{C}
and for each
g^{\prime},g\in G
, such that

•

(id)_{xy}
is the identity map, and

•

The action is compatible with composition: for every
x>y>z
objects of
\mathcal{C}
, writing
(f_{x,y,z},\bar{f}_{x,y,z})
for the composition
\mathcal{C}(x,y)\times\mathcal{C}(y,z)\to\mathcal{C}(x,z)
, we have that
g_{xz}\bar{f}_{x,y,z}=\bar{f}_{gx,xy,gz}\circ(g_{xy}\times g_{yz}).

Notation.
Given a group
G
acting some set
X
, we will denote the stabilizer of
x\in X
either by
G_{x}
or by
Stab_{x}
.

Definition 35
.

An integral action
\bar{A}
on a
G
-equivariant flow category is the same as the correspondig data for the underlying flow category, with the additional requirements that

\bar{A}(gx)=\bar{A}(x)\text{ and }\beta_{x,y}\circ\hat{i}^{g}_{xy}=\beta_{gx,gy}\text{ for any }g\in G,x,y\in Ob(\mathcal{C}).

Definition 36
.

A
grading
on a
G
-equivariant flow category
\mathcal{C}
is an assignment of a pair of vector spaces
(V^{0}(x),V^{1}(x))
to each object
x\in\mathcal{C}
, together with linear isomorphisms of inner product spaces
g^{0}_{x}:V^{0}(x)\to V^{0}(gx)
,
g^{1}_{x}:V^{1}(x)\to V^{1}(gx)
, for each
g\in G
and
x\in\mathcal{C}
, such that

(id)^{0}_{x}=id,(id)^{1}_{x}=id,(g^{\prime})^{0}_{gx}g^{0}_{x}=(g^{\prime}g)^{0}_{x},(g^{\prime})^{1}_{gx}g^{1}_{x}=(g^{\prime}g)^{1}_{x}.

An
isomorphism
of gradings

\{(V^{0}(x),V^{1}(x)),(g^{0}_{x},g^{1}_{x})\}\simeq\{(\hat{V}^{0},\hat{V}^{1});(\widehat{g^{0}_{x}},\widehat{g^{1}_{x}})\}

is an assignment of a vector spaces
W(x),\widehat{W}(x)
for each
x\in Ob(\mathcal{C})
together with isomorphisms
g^{w}_{x}:W(x)\to W(gx)
,
\widehat{g^{w}_{x}}:\widehat{W(x)}\to\widehat{W(gx)}
for each
x\in\mathcal{C},g\in G
satisfying

(id)^{w}_{x}=\widehat{(id)^{w}_{x}}=id,(g^{\prime})^{w}_{gx}g^{w}_{x}=(g^{\prime}g)^{w}_{x},\widehat{(g^{\prime})^{w}_{gx}}\widehat{g^{w}_{x}}=\widehat{(g^{\prime}g)^{w,i}_{x}},

together with isomorphsms

h^{0}_{x}:V^{0}(x)\oplus W(x)\to\widehat{V^{0}(x)}\oplus\widehat{W(x)}

h^{1}_{x}:V^{1}(x)\oplus W(x)\to\widehat{V^{1}(x)}\oplus\widehat{W(x)}

such that

(\widehat{g^{0}_{x}}\oplus\widehat{g^{w}_{x}})\circ h^{0}_{x}=h_{gx}(g^{0}_{x}\oplus g^{w}_{x}),

(\widehat{g^{1}_{x}}\oplus\widehat{g^{w}_{x}})\circ h^{1}_{x}=h_{gx}(g^{1}_{x}\oplus g^{w}_{x}).

Isomorphism classes of gradings are specified by an assignment of an element
[V^{0}(x)]-[V^{1}(x)]\in RO(H_{x})
of a virtual
H
-representation for every orbit
[x]\in Ob(\mathcal{C})/G
with stabilizer (up to conjugacy)
H_{x}\in G
. We will denote the function
classifying isomorphism classes of gradings by

[\mu]:Ob(\mathcal{C})\to\bigsqcup_{H\subset G}RO(H).

We have that
[\mu](x)\in RO(Stab_{x})
, and
[\mu](gx)=g_{*}([\mu](x))\in RO(gStab_{x}g^{-1})
.

Definition 37
.

A
pre-grading
has the same definition as a grading. However, we will use pre-gradings and gradings differently, and the comparison between the two will be through the (essentially auxiliary) integral action. Specifically, given a pre-grading

\{(V^{0}(x),V^{1}(x)),(g^{0}_{x},g^{1}_{x})\}\simeq\{(\hat{V}^{0},\hat{V}^{1})
on a finite
G
-equivariant flow category with integral action
\bar{A},
there is an associated grading
\{(\widehat{V^{0}(x)},\widehat{V^{1}(x)}),(\widehat{g^{0}_{x}},\widehat{g^{1}_{x}})\}
well defined up to canonical isomorpism. Choosing some
N
such that
\bar{A}(x)+N\geq 0
for all
x\in\mathcal{C}
, the associated grading is defined via

\widehat{V^{0}(x)}=\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x)+N}\oplus V^{0}(x),\widehat{V^{0}(x)}=\mathbb{R}^{\bar{A}(x)+N}\oplus V^{0}(x),\widehat{g^{0}_{x}}=id\oplus g^{0}_{x},\widehat{g^{1}_{x}}=id\oplus g^{1}_{x}.

Definition 38
.

A smooth
G
-equivariant flow category is a smooth flow category such that the maps
i^{g}_{x,y}
are maps in
Man^{\prime}
.

Definition 39
.

A virtually smooth
G
-equivariant flow category is a virtually smooth flow category
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
(without stabilizations), where
\mathcal{C}
is a
G
-equivariant flow category, such that we also have isomorphisms

in
DerMan^{\prime}_{sm,no-stab}
from
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x,y)
to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(gx,gy)
, which we write as

i^{g}_{x,y}=(\hat{i}^{g}_{x,y}:S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}\to S_{\mathcal{C}(gx,gy)},(\bar{i}^{g}_{x,y}:T(x,y)\to T(gx,gy),(\bar{i}^{\prime})^{g}_{x,y}:V(x,y)\to V(gx,gy))

which satisfy

•

i^{g^{\prime}}_{gx,gy}i^{g}_{x,y}=i^{g^{\prime}g}_{x,y}
for all
g,g^{\prime}\in G
;

•

i^{id}_{x,y}
is the identity map, and

•

The action

for the composition

.

on the fiber;

which acts by identity on the fiber;

for the map of vector bundles

covering

which acts by
g^{1}_{xy}
on the fiber; and finally

Then the following conditions must be satisfied:

\eta_{gxgy}\circ d\bar{i}^{g}_{xy}=(\underline{id}\boxplus\underline{g^{0}_{xy}})\circ\eta_{xy};

(38)

\mu^{g}_{xy}\circ\eta^{\prime}_{xy}=\eta^{\prime}_{gxgy}(\bar{i}^{\prime})^{g}_{xy};

and finally that the maps

commute with the

into the (equivariant)

of the framed equivariant flow category (thought of as a

The compatibility conditions

(g^{\prime})^{0}_{gxgy}g^{0}_{xy}=(g^{\prime}g)^{0}_{xy};(g^{\prime})^{1}_{gxgy}g^{1}_{xy}=(g^{\prime}g)^{1}_{xy}

(40)

Definition 41
.

A framing of an equivariant flow category is compatible with a pre-grading if the pre-grading can be enhanced to some
G-E_{2}
-parameterization with associated
G-F_{2}
-parameterization isomorphic to the underlying
G-F_{2}
-parameterization of the category. A framing of an equivariant flow category is compatible with a grading
\mathcal{G}
if it is compatible with some pre-grading
\mathcal{G}_{pre}
with associated grading
\mathcal{G}
.

Definition 42
.

An embedding of a framing of a
G
-equivariant virtually smooth flow category is a
G
-
E_{2}
-parameterization with associated
G-F_{2}
-parameterization isomorphic to the one underlying the framing of the category. In other words, an embedding of a framing is the
same thing
as a compatible (pre)-grading. We will largely use the language of embeddings of framings in the rest of the paper, but this relation is essential in the interpretation of the construction.

Definition 43
.

An embedding of a framing of a
G
-equivariant virtually smooth flow category is adapted if the underlying framing is adapted.

Definition 44
.

A
framed embedding
of a finite, adapted, virtually smooth
G
-equivariant flow category without stabilizations is an embedding of the underlying flow category such that

\iota_{gxgy}\circ\bar{i}^{g}_{xy}=(id\times\hat{\lambda}^{g}_{xy})\circ\iota_{xy}.

(41)

Definition 45
.

An
extension
of an embedding of a
G
-equivariant flow category
\mathcal{C}
is an embedding of the underlying flow category
\mathcal{C}
such that

e_{gx}\circ(id\times g^{0}_{x})_{*}=\left(\bigvee_{z\in M(\mathcal{C})}(g^{0}_{z}\oplus g^{1}_{x,z})_{*}\right)\circ e_{x}.

We now wish to define the genuine equivariant stable homotopy type associated to an extension of a framed embedding of a
G
-equivariant flow category.

Definition 46
.

The equivariant stable homotopy type asociated to an extension of an embedding of a virtually smooth
G
-equivariant flow category is given by the same formula as the definition of the stable homotopy type of the extension of the embedding the underlying virtually smooth flow category, using the fact that
G
acts on

\bigvee_{z\in\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\bar{A}(x)+1}\times V^{0}(x)\oplus V^{1}(x)^{\perp})_{*}

via

\bigvee_{z\notin M(\mathcal{C})}(id\times g^{0}_{x}\oplus(g^{1}_{x})^{\perp})_{*}

where
V^{1}_{max}
is naturally an orthogonal
G
-representation and

g^{1}_{x}:V^{1}(x)^{\perp}\to V^{1}(gx)^{\perp}

is induced by restricting the
G
action to
V^{1}(x)^{\perp}\subset V^{1}_{max}
.

It is clear from the construction that

Proposition 1
.

The spectrum
|\mathcal{C}|
underlying the equivariant stable homotopy type of
\mathcal{C}
is the stable homotopy type of
\mathcal{C}
thought of as a non-equivariant flow category.

Definition 47
.

Let
H\subset G
be a
normal
subgroup and let
\mathcal{C}
be a
G
-equivariant flow category. The fixed point flow category
\mathcal{C}^{H}
the
G/H
-equivariant flow category with objects
Ob(\mathcal{C})^{H}
and morphisms
\mathcal{C}^{H}(x,y)=\mathcal{C}(x,y)^{H}
.

If
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
is a virtually smooth
G
-equivariant flow category then
(\mathcal{C}^{H},(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})^{H})
is a virtually smooth
G/H
-equivariant flow category, where

(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})^{H}(x,y)=(T(x,y)^{H},V(x,y)^{H},\sigma(x,y)^{H})

where we define
\sigma(x,y)^{H}(c)=\frac{1}{|H|}\sum_{h\in H}h\sigma(x,y)(c)
for
c\in T(x,y)^{H}
; this is the canonical
H
-equivariant projection
V(x,y)_{c}\to V(x,y)_{c}^{H}
.

Definition 48
.

If
\mu=(V^{0}(x),V^{1}(x),g^{0}_{x},g^{1}_{x})
is a grading or a pre-grading on a
G
-equivariant flow category
\mathcal{C}
, then for any
x\in\mathcal{C}^{H}
, there is an associated grading or pre-grading on
\mathcal{C}^{H}
defined via

(V^{0}(x)^{H},V^{1}(x)^{H},[g]^{0}_{x}:=(g^{0}_{x})|_{V^{0}(x)^{H}},[g]^{1}_{x}:=(g^{1}_{x})|_{V^{1}(x)^{H}}).

Lemma 3
.

Given a framing of a virtually smooth
G
-equivariant flow category
\mathcal{C}
, there is an associated framing of
(\mathcal{C}^{H},(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})^{H})
defined by

V^{0}_{(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})^{H}}(x,y)=V^{0}_{(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})}(x,y)^{H},V^{1}_{(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})^{H}}(x,y)=V^{1}_{(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})}(x,y)^{H},W^{0}_{(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})^{H}}(x,y,z)=W^{0}_{(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})}(x,y,z)^{H}

together with

([g]^{0}_{xy})_{(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})^{H}}=(g_{xy})^{H},([g]^{1}_{xy})_{(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})^{H}}=(g^{1}_{xy})^{H}

where we use the fact that for
x>y>z\in\mathcal{C}^{H}
,

(g^{0}_{xy},g^{1}_{xy})_{g\in H}
make
(V^{0}(x,y),V^{1}(x,y),W^{0}(x,y,z))
into
H
-representations as
(x,y,z)=(gx,gy,gz)
.

Theorem 8
.

The geometric fixed points of the equivariant stable homotopy type associated to a flow category agree with the stable homotopy type of fixed point flow category:

|\mathcal{C}|^{\Phi H}=|\mathcal{C}^{H}|.

(42)

This follows from a point-set computation together with

Proposition 2
.

The space
Y_{\mathcal{C}}
of Definition
46
has the homotopy type of a
G
-CW complex.

Proof.

Note that
(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{k})_{*}=c(S^{k-1})=(\mathbb{R}_{+})_{*}\wedge S^{k-1}
. Using the partial ordering on
Ob(\mathcal{C})
given by
y\leq x
if
\mathcal{C}(x,y)\neq\emptyset
, we thus see that
Y_{\mathcal{C}}=Y^{r}
, where
Y^{0}=*
and
Y^{r}
is built from
Y^{r-1}
by forming the cone on a map

f_{r}:\wedge_{x\in[x]}S_{x}^{V^{\prime}}\to Y^{r-1}

where
[x]
is an orbit of the
G
-action on
Ob(\mathcal{C})
,
H
is the stabilzer of some arbitrary base-point
x_{0}\in[x]
,
V^{\prime}
is some
G/H
representation,
x
is simply a label on the sphere, and
g\in G
acts on
\wedge_{x\in[x]}S_{x}^{V^{\prime}}
by

\wedge_{x\in[x]}(g_{*}:S^{V^{\prime}}_{x}\to S^{V^{\prime}}_{gx}),

i.e. by permuting spheres as
G
permutes objects and acting on the corresponding representation spheres as in the representation
V^{\prime}
. Mapping cones on
G
-homotopy equivalent maps are
G
-homotopy equivalent; by induction we can assume that
Y_{r}
has the
G
-homotopy type of a
G
-CW complex, in which case the equivariant whitehead theorem implies that
f_{r}
is homotopy equivalent to a
G
-CW map and thus
Y_{r}
is
G
-homotopy equivalent to a
G
-CW complex as well.
∎

5
Flow Categories: Constructions

5.1
Equivariant embedings of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds

Definition 49
.

A
convenient metric
on a
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold
X
is a
G
-invariant metric for which all the boundary hypersurfaces
X_{j}
are totally geodesic and intersect totally orthogonally in the sense that for any
S\in[k]
and any
x\in X(S)
, any sequence of nonzero vectors
v_{j}\in T_{x}X(S)^{\perp}\subset T_{x}X(S\cup\{j\})
ranging over
j\notin S
is pairwise orthogonal in
T_{x}X
.

Lemma 4
.

Any
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold
X
admits a convenient metric
g
. Given convenient metrics
g_{i}
on
X_{i}
such that
g_{i}|_{X_{i}\cap X_{j}}=g_{j}|_{X_{i}\cap X_{j}}
(defining a metric
g_{0}
on
\partial X
) the convenient metric
g
can be chosen to be an extension of all
g_{i}
.

Proof.

We employ the
doubling construction
on
X
. This construction is a generalization to
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds of doubling construction on manifolds with boundary
Y
, which puts a smooth structure on
Y_{\cup\partial Y}Y
making the latter into a closed
\mathbb{Z}/2
-manifold, such that the inclusion of
Y\to Y\cup_{\partial Y}Y
is a smooth embedding. The analog for
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds
X
puts a smooth structure on the union

\mathcal{D}(X)=\bigsqcup_{i\in 2^{k}}X/\sim

of
2^{k}
copies of
X
glued appropriately along their strata
X(S)
, such that
\mathcal{D}(X)
is a
G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-manifold and the inclusion of one copy of
X
is the fundamental domain of the
1\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
action. The details of this construction are worked out in
[
85
]
, although it is implicit in
[
12
]
.

We then apply an equivariant embedding theorem

[
97
]
that (for any compact Lie group

G
) every
G
-manifold
Y
can be isometrically embedded into
an orthogonal
G
-representation
V
, and if such an embedding
was specified on a closed subset then it can be extended to the
whole manifold after enlarging
V
to a larger
G
-
representation. Moreover, the
G
-representation
V
can be
chosen to be
subordinate
to the
G
action on
Y
, which
means that
V
is a direct sum of irreducible
G
-
representations
V_{1},\ldots,V_{r}
such that for each
m\in M

with stabilizer
G_{m}\subset G
, the manifold
G/G_{m}
embeds
equivariantly into
(\oplus_{i=1}^{r}V_{i})^{\ell}
for some
\ell
,
as does the
G_{m}
-representation
T_{x}M/Lie(G_{m})

[
97
, Proposition 1.7]
. The
assumptions of the theorem give a
G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-
equivariant metric
g_{0}
on the
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-orbit
\mathcal{D}(\partial X)\subset\mathcal{D}(X)
of
\partial X\subset X
. Repeatedly applying the previous theorem
allows us to define an embedding of
\mathcal{D}(X)
into some
(G\times\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-representation
V
which is an isometry on
\mathcal{D}(\partial X)\subset\mathcal{D}(X)
. Pulling the metric on
V
back onto
\mathcal{D}(X)
defines an equivariant extension of
g_{0}
, and restricting this metric to
X
defines the metric required in the theorem. Indeed, the boundary hypersurfaces
X_{j}
will be totally geodesic in
X
because the images of
X_{j}
in
V
are the the intersections of the image of
X
with the totally geodesic submanifolds given by the subrepresentations
V_{j}\subset V
on which the
j
-th
\mathbb{Z}/2
factors act trivially. The condition on the orthogonality holds because it holds for any
Stab(x)\subset G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-invariant inner product on
T_{x}\mathcal{D}(X)=T_{x}X
.
∎

One would like to use the equivariant embedding theorem quoted above and the doubling construction to produce embeddings of
G-\langle k\rangle
manifolds into
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}\times\hat{V}
for some
G
-representation
V
. Unfortunately, the proof of the equivariant embedding theorem does not make it immediately clear that one can choose the representation on which
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
acts nontrivially to be precisely the representation
\mathbb{R}^{k}
on which the
i
-th
\mathbb{Z}/2
in
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
acts by flipping the
i
-th coordinate. As such, we give a direct proof of this fact.

Lemma 5
.

Let
\hat{V}
be a
G
-representation, and let
Y
be a
G-\langle k\rangle
manifold with a convenient metric. Suppose that one has equivariant immersions

\iota_{j}:Y_{j}\to(\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+})_{j}\times\hat{V}

such that for any
S^{\prime}\subset[k]
, the restrictions of
\iota_{j}
for all
j\in[k]\setminus S^{\prime}
to
Y(S)
agree and factor through
(\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+})(S)\times\hat{V}
. Suppose one also has a
G
-equivariant framing

\eta:TX\simeq\underline{T\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}\times V}

for some
G
-representation
V
.

Then there exists a
G
-representation
\hat{V}^{\prime}
together with an immersion

\iota:Y\to\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}\times\hat{V}\times\hat{V^{\prime}}

which maps the top stratum of
Y(S^{\prime})
to the top stratum of
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}(S^{\prime})\times\hat{V}\oplus\hat{V^{\prime}}
for all
S^{\prime}\subset[k]
, is an embedding on the top open stratum of
Y
, and satisfies
\iota|_{Y_{j}}=\iota_{j}\times 0
.

Moreover, if
X
has a convenient metric, then if the composition of
\iota_{j}
with the projection to the
r
-th factor of
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}
is the distance on
Y_{j}
from
Y_{j}\cap Y_{r}
in a neighborhood of the latter for every
j,r
, and and the projection of
d\iota_{j}
onto
T(\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+})_{j}
is full rank everywhere, then we can choose the composition of
\iota
with the projection to the
r
-th factor of
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}
to be the distance from
Y_{r}
for every
r
, with the differential full rank everywhere.

This latter condition implies that

d\iota(TY)^{\perp}\cap T(\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+})_{j}\times T\hat{V}=(d\iota_{j})(TY_{j})^{\perp}.

Proof.

In a small neighborhood of
Y_{j}
, we choose the map to the
j
-th coordinate of
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}
be the distance to
Y_{j}
, and the other coordinates are given by composition to the projection to
Y_{j}
with the corresponding component of
\iota_{j}
. This defines a submersion to
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}
on an open neighborhood of
\partial Y_{j}
, which we then extend to a submersion mapping the rest of
Y
to the interior of
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}
using the relative
h
-principle for holonomic approximation
[
40
]
(this uses the condition regarding the existence of the framing
\eta
). We define the map to
\hat{V}
via an arbitrary smooth extension of the compositions of
\iota_{j}
with the projection
\hat{V}
. To define the map to
\hat{V}^{\prime}
, we follow the standard proof of the equivariant embedding theorem.
∎

5.2
Parameterizations of framings

When defining the framing of a virtually smoooth flow category (Definition
23
), we are forced to introduce maps
\tau_{xyz}
(
22
) which relate the vector spaces serving as the targets of the trivializations of the tangent bundles
TT(x,y)
various thickenings
T(x,y)
, in order to make sense of the compatibility conditions between the framings of these tangent bundles. Moreover, we will see that when working with virtually smooth flow categories, we will be forced to
stabilize
the Kuranishi charts comprising the flow category in a
compatible
way. This linear algebra data organizing the compatibility conditions can be challenging to work with, especially as one changes groups and performs various stabilizations. In particular, when comparing two
different
virtually smooth flow categories, one is forced to
compare
this linear algebra data, which in general is a combinatorically elaborate process. In this section, we introduce some terminology regarding different ways of organizing this linear algebra data, and prove some helpful results. In particular, we introduce the notion of a
free parameterization
(Definition
60
) which is
fundamental
to the arguments used in this paper. Free parameterizations are completely controlled by their associated numerical data (Lemma
12
), and freeness is preserved under group restriction, making free parameterizations easy to work with. A mild extension of this notion is a
semi-free
parameterization, which is a direct sum of a free parameterization and a canonical parameterization, and has the same convenient properties as free parameterizations.
All virtually smooth flow categories in this paper will be have framings parameterized by semi-free parameterizations.
When writing this paper, we found that the systematic use of (semi-)free parameterizations dramatically simplified various constructions with flow categories, avoiding many technical difficulties that arose with other approaches.

Representations of posets.

Let
\Gamma
be finite poset. We will write
x\in\Gamma
to denote that
x
is an object of
\Gamma
.

Definition 50
.

A representation
\mathcal{E}
of
\Gamma
is a assignment of inner product spaces
\{V(x)\}_{x\in\Gamma}
, together with isometric embeddings
\hat{\alpha}_{xy}:V(y)\to V(x)
for each
x>y
such that if
x>y>z
then the embedding of
V(z)\to V(x)
agrees with the embedding
V(x)\to V(y)\to V(z)
. In other words, a representation
\mathcal{E}
of
\Gamma
is a functor from
\Gamma
to the category of inner product spaces and isometric embeddings of such. A morphism of representations is a natural transformation of functors.

We will often refer to a representation of
\Gamma
via the notation
\{V(x)\}_{x\in\Gamma}
, and supressing the mention of the maps
\hat{\alpha}_{xy}
.

Definition 51
.

A
free representation
of
\Gamma
is a representation of
\Gamma
satisfying the following condition. For
x\in\Gamma
, write
V(x)^{*}\subset V(x)
to the perpendicular of the sum of the images of all
V(y)
for
y<x
. A representation of
\Gamma
is free when

V(x)=\oplus_{x\geq y}V(y)^{*}

(43)

where we have identified
V(y)^{*}
with its image in
V(x)
under the embedding
V(y)\to V(x)
.

Definition 52
.

Given a representation
\{V(x)\}
of
\Gamma
, a stabilization of
\{V(x)\}
is a representation isomorphic to some representation
\{V^{\prime}(x)\}
, where

V^{\prime}(x)=V(x)\oplus\bigoplus_{y\leq x}\tilde{V}(y)

(44)

for some arbitrary choice of vector spaces
\{\tilde{V}(y)\}_{y\in\Gamma}
, with the inclusions
V^{\prime}(y)\to V^{\prime}(x)
for
x>y
given by the direct sum of the inclusion map
V(x)\to V(y)
with the natural inclusions of the
\tilde{V}(y)
into the direct sum. When the vector spaces
\tilde{V}(y)
are nonzero exactly for
y\in\Gamma^{\prime}\subset\Gamma
we say that this is a stabilization
at

\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Let
G
be a finite group.

Definition 53
.

A
G
-poset is a poset
\Gamma
with an action of
G
on the objects of
\Gamma
, such that if
x\geq y
then
gx\geq gy
.

Definition 54
.

A representation of a
G
-poset is a representation
\{V(x)\}_{x\in\Gamma}
of the underlying poset, together with isometries

g_{x}:V(x)\to V(gx)
for every
g\in G
and
x\in\Gamma
such that

h_{gx}g_{x}=(hg)_{x}\text{ for }g,h\in G,x\in\Gamma,id_{x}=id,\text{ and }

(45)

g_{x}\hat{\alpha}_{xy}=\hat{\alpha}_{gx,gy}g_{y}\text{ for }g\in G,x,y\in\Gamma,\text{ and }x>y,

(46)

where
\hat{\alpha}_{xy}:V(y)\to V(x)
is the map associated to the representation of the underlying poset.

Definition 55
.

A representation of a
G
-poset is free when it is free as a representation of the underlying poset. A stabilization of a representation
\{V(x)\}
of a
G
-poset is defined in the same way as a stabilization of the representation of the underlying poset, but for which the action of
g_{x}
on
V^{\prime}(x)
resticts to the action of
g_{x}
on
V(x)
and sends
\tilde{V}(x)
isometrically to
\tilde{V}(gx)
in the decompositions of (
44
).

Definition 56
.

A direct sum
\mathcal{V}\oplus\mathcal{W}
of representations
\mathcal{V}=\{V(x)\},\mathcal{W}=\{W(x)\}
of a
G
-poset
\Gamma
assigns to
x\in\Gamma
the vector space
V(x)\oplus W(x)
and the
x\geq y
the direct sum of the embeddings associated by
\mathcal{V}
and by
\mathcal{W}
.

The following lemma is immediate:

Lemma 6
.

Stabilization of a representation of a
G
-poset
\Gamma
is the same as direct sum with a corresponding free representation of
\Gamma
. Free representations of a
G
-poset are exactly the stabilizations of the zero representation.

\blacksquare

Finally, there is a convenient way of constructing stabilizations of a
G
-poset:

Lemma 7
.

Given a
G
-poset
\Gamma
, a choice of
x\in\Gamma
, and a
G_{x}
-representation
\tilde{V}(x)
, there is a free representation of
\Gamma
which is, as a representation of the underlying poset, the stabilization of the zero representation exactly at the
G
-orbit of
x
. This free representation of the
G
-poset is unique up to isomorphism.

Proof.

All elements of the
G
-orbit of
x
are necessarily incomparable. The construction of the desired free representation then follows from the construction of induced representations: we choose representatives
g_{1},\ldots,g_{r}
of
G/G_{x}
, and we ask for the the representation to assign
g_{i}\tilde{V}(x)
to
g_{i}x
. If
g_{i}x=x^{\prime}
and
gx^{\prime}=g_{j}x
, then set the maps
g_{x^{\prime}}:g_{i}\tilde{V}(x)\to g_{j}\tilde{v}(x)
for
x^{\prime}\in G_{x}
to be such that
\oplus_{x^{\prime}\in Gx}g_{x^{\prime}}
is the action of
g
on the induced representation
\ind_{G_{x}}^{G}\tilde{V}(x)
. The uniqueness up to isomorphism of induced representations translates into the correspondig statement for this free representation.
∎

Parameterizations of framings.

Definition 57
.

An
E^{\prime}
-parameterization associated to an (equivariant) flow category
\mathcal{C}
is a representation of the underlying poset of
