Remark 52
.

If
X
a
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold with a boundary-smooth structure, then by Proposition
13
,
Coll(X)\times V
has a smooth structure which restricts to the smooth structures on
Coll(X)(S)\times V
for
S\subset[k]
,
S\neq[k]
. By repeatedly using the collaring construction, we could prove an analog of Proposition
15
without Proposition
12
, and proceed with the rest of the paper. However, this would involve collaring
T_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)}(x,y)
many times, which makes the statement of the analog of Proposition
15
somewhat inelegant, and one would be forced to prove a number of invariance results associated to the effect of collaring a flow category on downstream constructions, e.g. on the Floer homotopy type, would would add a similar amount of complexity. The strategy of repeatedly collaring flow categories is taken in previous works
[
5
,
85
,
20
]
and we avoid this once and for all with the propositions of this section.

Lemma 27
.

Let
Y
be a smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold, and let
y\in Y(S)
lie in the interior of
Y(S)
and
S\subset[k]
,
S\neq[k]
. A smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold boundary-chart at
y
is a map

\psi:\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\setminus(\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+})^{\circ}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\to Y

where
(\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+})^{\circ}
denotes the interior of
\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}
, satisfying the following properties:

•

For
y\in V
, we have that
S(y)\geq S(\psi(0,0))
, and moreover

•

For
[k]\supset T\supset S
with
T\neq S
, have that
\psi^{-1}(X(T))\subset\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}(r_{S}(T))\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
.

Then the restriction of
\psi
to some neighborhood of
0
is the restriction of a smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold chart at
y
to
\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\setminus(\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+})^{\circ}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
.

Moreover, if
Y
is a
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold where
G
is finite, then if
\psi
is
Stab(x)
-equivariant for a
Stab(x)
on the domain which is the product of the trivial action on
\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\setminus(\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+})^{\circ}
and a faithful linear action on
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, then the extending smooth-manifold chart can be taken to be
Stab(x)
-equivariant as well, with domain neighborhood of zero in
\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\setminus(\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+})^{\circ}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
with the trivial
Stab(x)
on the first factor and the same linear action on the second factor.

Proof.

Write
\partial\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}=\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\setminus(\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+})^{\circ}.

Let
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\sup U\to Y
be a smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold chart at
y
. After shrinking
U
, there is a map

\psi^{-1}\circ\phi:\partial\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\to V\supset\partial\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}

for some neighborhood
V
of
U
. The components of this map define functions

f_{i}:\partial\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\cap U\to V\to\mathbb{R}_{+},i\in\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}

as well as a function

g:\partial\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}

such that both
g
and
f_{j}
are smooth when restricted to each stratum
\partial\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}([k]\setminus j)\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\cap U
for
j\in[k]\setminus S
, and that
f_{j}^{-1}(0)=\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}([k]\setminus j)\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
, with
df_{j}(x)
nonzero when
x\in\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}(T)\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
for
T\subsetneq[k]\setminus j
. Write
g_{y}:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
for the restriction of
g
to
\{y\}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
. Then we have that
dg_{0}\neq 0
. Thus, by the inverse function theorem, after possibly shrinking
U
, if we extend
g
to a smooth function
U\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
f_{i}
to smooth functions
U\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
f_{j}^{-1}(0)=(\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}([k]\setminus j)\times\mathbb{R}^{m})\cap U
, with
df_{j}|_{f_{j}^{-1}(0)\neq 0}
then the inverse function theorem lets us extend a restriction of
\psi
to neighborhood of
0
to a smooth
\langle k\rangle
-chart around
0
.

The statement that
g
can be equivariantly extended to a smooth function is standard, see for example
[
85
]
. To extend each
f_{j}
to smooth functions with the desired properties, we note that we can write
f_{j}=x_{j}\tilde{f}_{j}
for
x_{j}
the coordinate which is zero on
\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}([k]\setminus j)\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
\tilde{f}_{j}\neq 0
on a neighborhood of
\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}([k]\setminus j)\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
. Thus after extending
\tilde{f}_{j}
to
U
and defining
f_{j}=x_{j}\tilde{f}_{j}
on
U
, after possibly shrinking
U
we will have an extension of
f_{j}
with the desired properties.
∎

Lemma 28
.

Given a topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
X
with a boundary-smooth structure and a point
x\in X(S)
with
x
lying in the interior of
X(S)
, there exists a topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold chart
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\to X
,
m=\dim X-k+|S|
, such that
\psi
is smooth restricted to any boundary hypersurface of its domain. Moreover, this chart can be taken to be
Stab(x)
equivariant with the action on the domain being trivial on the first factor and linear and faithful on the second factor.

Proof.

Repeated application of Lemma
27
allows us to construct maps

\psi:\partial\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\to X

satisfying the following properties:

•

\psi((0,0))=x
;

•

For
y\in V
, we have that
S(y)\geq S(\psi((0,0))
, and moreover

•

For
[k]\supset T\supset S
with
T\neq S
, have that
\psi^{-1}(X(T))\subset\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}(r_{S}(T))\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
and finally

•

\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{P}([k]\setminus j)\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\to X([k]\setminus j)
is a smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold chart at
x
, for each
j\in[k]\setminus S
.

Given the above data, the lemma follows from an application of the tubular neighborhood theorem for topological manifolds with boundary: using the fact that topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds are topological manifolds with boundary, one can produce a global topological collar on
X
, and a use a choice of topological collar on
\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
thought of as a topological manifold with boundary to define an extension of
\psi
to an open neighborhood of
(0,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
which is a topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold chart at
x
.
∎

Proposition 17
.

Let
X
be a
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold with a boundary-smooth structure. Choose a boundary-hypersurface
X_{j}\subset X
,
j\in[k]
. There exists a map

\phi_{j}:X_{j}\times[0,1]\cup_{X_{j}}X\to X

(where we identify
X_{j}\times\{1\}
on the left with
X_{j}\subset X
on the right) which is a homeomorphism onto its image, such that

•

\phi_{j}|_{X_{j}(S)\times\{0\}}
is a diffeomorphism onto
X(S)
for
S\subset[k]\setminus j
;

•

For
k\neq j
,

\phi_{j}|_{k}:X_{j}\cap X_{k}\times[0,1]\cup_{X_{j}\cap X_{k}}X_{k}\to X_{k}

is a diffeomorphism onto its (open) image, and

•

\phi_{j}
is
G
-equivariant.

Moreover, if
X
has a nice
G-\langle k\rangle
-submanifold with a smooth structure an an elegant metric, then
\phi
can be chosen to be elegant along
Y\cap X_{j}
, i.e
\phi_{j}(Y_{j}\times[0,1]\cup_{Y_{j}}Y)\subset Y
, and the metric on the image of
\phi_{j}:Y_{j}\times[0,1]
is the product of the induced metric on
\phi_{j}(Y_{j})
with the metric on an interval.

Proof of Proposition
12
.

This follows by repeatedly applying Proposition
17
. For
r=1,2,\ldots,k
, define

\mathcal{S}_{r}\subset 2^{[k]},S_{r}=2^{[k]\setminus 1}\cup 2^{[k]\setminus 2}\cup\ldots\cup 2^{[k]\setminus r}.

Define

Coll_{r}(X)\subset Coll(X)

Coll_{k}(X)=\cup_{T\in\mathcal{S}_{r}}X(T)\times[0,1]^{[k]\setminus T}/\sim.

Note that
Coll_{1}(X)=Coll(X_{1})\times[0,1].
Let us imagine first that
Y
is not present. Then,
applying Proposition
17
to
j=1
and composing
\phi_{1}
with the collar
Coll(X_{1})\to X_{1}
defines a map
f_{1}:Coll_{1}(X)\to X
which is stratum preserving and is a diffeomorphism onto its image when restricted to each boundary stratum. This is the first step of the induction. For
1<r\leq k
, assume that we have defined a map
f_{r-1}:Coll_{r-1}(X)\to X
which is a diffeomorphism onto its image when restricted to each boundary stratum. Invoking Proposition
17
for
j=r
we get a map
\phi_{r}
as in the proposition. This map in turn defines
\bar{\phi}_{r}
via the composition
X\to X\times[0,1]\cup X\xrightarrow{\phi}_{r}X
, where the first arrow is the inclusion into the second term of the union. Now note that
Coll_{r}(X)=Coll_{r-1}(X)\cup Coll_{r-1}(X)_{r}\times[0,1]
. We define
f_{r}:Coll_{r}(X)\to X
to be
\bar{\phi}_{r}\circ f_{r-1}
on
Coll_{r-1}(X)
, and
f_{r}|_{Coll_{r-1}(X)_{r}\times[0,1]}
to be
\phi_{r}(f_{r-1}\times id)
. The variant where
Y
is present is straightforward via the corresponding variant of Proposition
17
(choosing
\phi_{j}
such that
\phi_{j}(X_{j}\times[0,1])
lies in a sufficiently small neighborhood of
X_{j}
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
17
.

This follows from the argument of
[
33
]
. We use the notation of that paper in this proof. We note first that
X_{r}
is locally collared in
X
for any
1\leq r\leq k
. Moreover, our spaces are paracompact Hausdorff topological manifolds with boundary, so every open cover admits a star-finite open refinement
[
47
]
. We choose the open sets
U_{1},\ldots,U_{n}
as in the proof
[
33
]
so that
\overline{V}\subset U_{1}
and such that
h_{2},\ldots,h_{n}
arise from local charts produced as in Lemma
28
. Moreover we require that
G
permutes the neighborhoods
U_{2},\ldots,U_{n}
and acts freely on this set, and moreover such that if
gU_{a}=U_{b}
then
h_{b}\circ g=h_{a}
. We then find a star-finite refinement
V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}
such that
\overline{V}_{i}\subset U_{i}
and such that
\overline{V}\subset V_{1}
. The maps
f_{1}
and
g_{1}
as in
[
33
]
is defined using
\tilde{\phi}_{j}
. For each inductive step, we require that
\phi_{i}
is
Stab(U_{i})
-equivariant and such that
\phi_{i}|_{H_{i}g_{i-1}(X_{k})}
is a diffeomorphism for each
k\neq j
; this can be achieved by using the smooth equivariant Tietze theorem to define
\lambda_{i}
on
\overline{U}_{i}\cap\partial X
first and then extending using the equivariant Tietze extension theorem. After defining
\phi_{i}
one defines
\phi_{b}
for all
b
such that
U_{b}=gU_{i}
by
G
-equivariance, and then proceeds onwards with the induction choosing the first
U_{\ell}
such that
\phi_{\ell}
has not yet been defined. The variant with
Y
present just involves using the local collarings to be elegant near
Y
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
15
.

This follows from the equivariant double induction as in Propositions
3
or
7
. The outer loop of the induction is on downwards closed
G
-subposets
\mathcal{P}\subset\mathcal{C}(H,J)
. The inductive hypothesis is that there is an
E
-parameterization
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}
with associated
F
-parameterization
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}
such that we have chosen a smooth lift of the full subcategory
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}};\mathcal{P}}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}}
on the objects of
\mathcal{P}
. To expand
\mathcal{P}
we to
\mathcal{P}_{x}
choose a minimal
x\in\mathcal{C}(H,J)\setminus\mathcal{P}
as usual, and introduce
\mathcal{P}_{x},\mathcal{P}_{\bar{x}},
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
as usual. Clearly
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}};\mathcal{P}_{x}}
is a
G_{x}
-equivariant topologically smooth flow category. Over one run of the inner loop, we will define an
E
-parameterization
\mathcal{E}_{x}
which is the stabilization along the
G
-orbit of
x
of the zero
E
-parameterization (Lemma
7
). Write
\mathcal{F}_{x}
for the associated
F
-parameterization; note that for
w,w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, we have that the vector space associated by
\mathcal{F}_{x}
to the pair
(w,w^{\prime})
is nonzero exactly if
w\in Gx
, and in the latter case is exactly the vector space associated by
\mathcal{E}_{x}
to
w
. We then set
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}}=\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{x}
,
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}}=\mathcal{F}\oplus\mathcal{F}_{x}
. One run of the inner loop will also define a smooth lift of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}};\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
which agrees with the previously chosen smooth lift of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}};\mathcal{P}}=\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}};\mathcal{P}}
. This smooth lift of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}}
is defined by transport of structure (i.e. by the
G/G_{x}
-action) via a choice of smooth lift of the
G_{x}
-equivariant topologically smooth flow category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}};\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{x}}
, which in turn only depends on the
G_{x}
-representation
\tilde{V}(x)
that
\mathcal{E}_{x}
assigns to
x
, which itself defines
\mathcal{E}_{x}
.

To choose this latter smooth lift and to define the representation
\tilde{V}(x)
, we induct downwards on upwards-closed
G_{x}
-subposets
\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
. At each step we will enlarge
\tilde{V}(x)
; for shorthand, write
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime};\tilde{V}(x)}
for
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P^{\prime}}}
defined via this choice of
\tilde{V}(x)
. The inductive assumption is that we have chosen a smooth lift of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime};\mathcal{Q}}}
which agrees with the smooth lifts chosen in previous iterations of the outer loop. We will write
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime};\mathcal{Q}}
for the corresponding
F
-parameterization with obtained from
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}
by stabilizing by
\tilde{V}(x)
(with its value at the end of the step corresponding to
\mathcal{Q}
) at
x
. We will enlarge
\mathcal{Q}
to
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}=\mathcal{Q}\cup\{G_{x}y\}
with
y
maximal in
\mathcal{P}_{x}\setminus\mathcal{Q}
as usual. At the end of the step let us denote the new value of
\tilde{V}(x)
by
\tilde{V}(x)^{\prime}
; we will simply set
\tilde{V}(x)^{\prime}=\tilde{V}(x)\oplus R
where
R
is some sufficiently large
G_{x}
-representation. To choose
R
, we note that the thickening associated to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime};\tilde{V}(x)}}(x,y)
is a topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold with a boundary-smooth structure.

We now wish to apply Proposition
14
to give a stabilization of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime};\mathcal{Q}}}(x,y)
a smooth structure. We have that this thickening has a microbundle lift
h
coming from Lemma
24
; the previous steps of this induction will define homotopies
h^{j}_{t}
of the restrictions of this microbundle lift to the boundary strata of the thickening, satisfying the conditions of Lemma

26
, and such that
h^{j}_{1}
is smooth on the
j
-th boundary face of the thickening with respect to its smooth structure. Using Lemma
26
we extend this homotopy to a homotopy
h_{t}
of microbundle lifts with
h_{0}=0
, such that the new microbundle lift
h^{1}
is boundary-smooth in that it satisfies the conditions of Lemma
25
. We now use Proposition
14

with
X^{\prime}
chosen to be a neighborhood of the zero locus of the Kuranishi section of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}}(x,y)
; this implicitly defined further homotopy of the vector bundle lift
h^{1}
to a vector bundle lift which is smooth with respect to this smooth structure; moreover, this homotopy is constant on the boundary strata of the thickening. We use these homotopies in the later induction steps when utilizing Lemma
26
We now set
R
to be the representation produced by this application Proposition
14
applied to this thickening and this choice of
X^{\prime}
.

The result of Proposition
14
then defines a
G_{xy}
-equivariant smoothing of the thickening of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime};\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}}(x,y)
, which induces corresponding smoothings of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime};\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}}(x,y^{\prime})
for
y^{\prime}\in Gy
via transport of structure (using the
G_{x}
action). But this is exactly the data needed to fulfill the inductive hypothesis for
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\supset\mathcal{Q}
.

We have explained the inductive steps for both the inner and the outer inductions; each induction terminates by the finiteness of the number of objects of
\mathcal{C}(H,J)
, and the base cases can be taken to be vacuous. Note that we can use Definition
83
to interpret the stabilization of the virtual smoothing required as arising from a stabilization of the perturbation data.
∎

7.7
Stabilization

Given a surjective Fredholm operator
F:B_{1}\to B_{2}
, between a pair of Banach spaces, and another map
A:W\to B_{2}
where
W
is a finite-dimensional inner product space , there is an associated operator

F^{W}:V_{1}\oplus W\to V_{2}
by
F^{W}(v,w)=Dv-Aw
. Then
\ker D^{A}
is canonically isomorphic to
\ker D\oplus W
as follows: there is an exact sequence

0\to\ker D\xrightarrow{i}\ker D^{A}\xrightarrow{p}W\to 0

where
i
is induced by the inclusion
V_{1}=V_{1}\oplus 0\subset V_{1}\oplus W
, and
p
is induced by the projection
V_{1}\oplus W\to W
. In the case when all these spaces are inner product spaces with
i
an isometry, the map
p
has a
canonical
section (by sending
w
to the unique pair
(v,w)
such that
Dv+Aw=0
and such that
v
is perpendicular to
\ker D
). This canonical section induces a canonical isometry
W\to(\ker D)^{\perp}\subset\ker D^{A}
by using the polar decomposition.

Proposition 18
.

Let

(\mathfrak{P}_{1}=(\bar{\mathfrak{A}},\mathcal{V}^{1},\{\lambda_{V^{1}(x,y)}\})
be a regular equivariant system of perturbation data, and let
\mathfrak{P}_{2}=(\bar{\mathfrak{A}},\mathcal{V}^{2},\{\lambda_{V^{1}_{2}(x,y)}\})
be an equivariant system of perturbation data such that

\mathcal{V}_{2}=\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{F}

where
\mathcal{F}
is some
F^{\prime}
-parameterization of
\mathcal{C}(H,J)
, and such that

(\lambda_{V^{1}_{2}(x,y)})|_{V^{1}(x,y)}=\lambda_{V^{1}(x,y)}\text{ for all }x>y\text{ in }\mathcal{C}(H,J).

Denote the corresponding topological flow categories by
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}(H,J)
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}(H,J)
. Then a restriction of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}(H,J)
is the stabilization by
\mathcal{F}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}(H,J)
.

Definition 82
.

With the notation of Proposition
18
, we say that
\mathfrak{P}_{2}
is the stabilization of
\mathfrak{P}_{1}
by the data
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}=(\{V^{1}(x,y)^{\perp}\},\{\tau_{xyz}\}),\{\lambda^{1}_{2}(x,y)|_{V^{1}(x,y)^{\perp}}\})
, which we may refer to as an
F^{\prime}
-parameterization equipped with perturbation data.

Definition 83
.

With the notation of Definition
82
, we say that
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}
is equipped with
trivial
perturbation data when all the maps
\lambda^{1}_{2}(x,y)|_{V^{1}(x,y)^{\perp}}
are identically zero. As such, a stabilization of
\mathcal{P}_{1}
by
(\{V^{1}(x,y)^{\perp}\},\{\tau_{xyz}\}),\{0\})
is entirely determined by the underlying
F^{\prime}
-parameterization
\mathcal{F}=(\{V^{1}(x,y)^{\perp}\},\{\tau_{xyz}\}))
, and we will call
\mathcal{P}_{2}
the stabilization of
\mathcal{P}_{1}
by
\mathcal{F}
. One sees immediately from the definitions that in this setting, there is no restriction needed to get an isomorphism of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}(H,J)
with the stabilization of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}(H,J)
by
\mathcal{F}
, and the proof of Proposition
18
is essentially trivial.

Proof of Proposition
18
.

Write
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}(H,J)(x,y)=(T_{i}(x,y),\underline{V^{1}_{i}(x,y)},\sigma_{i}(x,y))
for
i=1,2
. There is an inclusion
T_{1}(x,y)\subset T_{2}(x,y)
for every
x>y
objects of
\mathcal{C}(H,J)
. Moreover, there are fiberwise smooth structures for the topological submersions
\pi_{i}:T_{i}(x,y)\to{\overline{M}}(x,y)
, and the
C^{1}_{loc}
structure on these submersions shows that the union of the fiberwise normal bundles defines a bundle

NT_{1}(x,y)=\cup_{a\in{\overline{M}}(x,y)}N(T_{1}(x,y))_{a}

over
T_{1}(x,y)
.

There is a continuous map
T_{2}(x_{-},x_{+})\to V^{1}(x,y)^{\perp}
given by the composition

(u,a,(v_{1},v_{2}))\mapsto v_{2}.

One sees immediately that this map is smooth on each fiber, and its fiberwise differential defines a map

T_{v}T_{2}(x_{-},x_{+})\to V^{1}(x,y)^{\perp}

which is fiberwise linear.
The
C^{1}_{loc}
charts show that this map is continuous. Moreover, by Lemma
24
and the surjectivity of
\mathcal{P}_{1}
we see that this map is surjective when restricted to each fiber, of
T_{v}T_{2}(x_{-},x_{+})
, and has kernel
T_{v}T_{1}(x_{-},x_{+})
. As such, one has a bundle isomorphism
NT_{1}(x_{-},x_{+})\simeq\underline{V^{1}(x,y)}^{\perp}
; write
r_{x_{-},x_{+}}
for the inverse of this bundle isomorphism. Over the image of
T_{1}(x_{-},z)\times T_{1}(z,x_{+})
in
T_{1}(x_{-},x_{+})
, there are canonical decompositions

T_{v}T_{i}(x_{-},z)\boxplus T_{v}T_{i}(z,x_{+})\simeq T_{v}T_{i}(x_{-},x_{+})\text{ for }i=1,2,

(99)

and the compatibility condition for perturbation data implies that
r_{x_{-},x_{+}}\underline{\tau^{\prime}_{x_{-},z,x_{+}}}=r_{x_{-},z}\oplus r_{z,x_{+}}
. Now, the bundles
T_{v}T_{i}(x_{-},x_{+})
all carry inner products induced by sum of the
L^{2}
inner product on rapidly decaying sections of
u^{*}TM
over
Z
and the
L^{2}
inner products on
V^{1}(x_{-},x_{+})
and
V^{1}_{2}(x_{-},x_{+})
. Thus the decompositions (
99
) are actually orthogonal. In particular, defining
r^{\prime}_{x_{-},x_{+}}
to be the bundle isometry
NT_{1}(x_{-},x_{+})\simeq\underline{V^{1}(x,y)}^{\perp}
produced by applying polar decomposition to
r_{x_{-},x_{+}}
, we have that
r^{\prime}_{x_{-},x_{+}}\underline{\tau^{\prime}_{x_{-},z,x_{+}}}=r^{\prime}_{x_{-},z}\oplus r^{\prime}_{z,x_{+}}
.

The above data inner products on the
T_{v}T_{i}(x_{-},x_{+})
defines Riemannian metrics on the (smooth) fibers of
T_{-}(x_{-},x_{+})
over
{\overline{M}}(x_{-},x_{+})
(here the smooth structure comes from the description of the the thickenings as products of zero sets of Fredholm sections over Banach manifolds,
not
via the previously described smooth structures!) such that the restriction of the Riemannian metrics on the fibers of
T_{2}(x_{-},x_{+})
agree with those on the fibers of
T_{1}(x_{-},x_{+})
, and such that the induced Riemannian metrics on the normal bundles
NT_{1}(x_{-},x_{+})
are those given by restricting the metrics on
T_{v}T_{2}(x_{-},x_{+})|_{T_{1}(x_{-},x_{+})}
to the perpendicular to
T_{v}T_{1}(x_{-},x_{+})
. Moreover, the charts associated to the gluing construction show that these Riemannian metrics vary continuously from fiber to fiber.

Putting all this together, we see that by applying the tubular neighborhood theorem to the inclusion
T_{1}(x_{-},x_{+})\subset T_{2}(x_{-},x_{+})
fiberwise that the restriction of the fiberwise exponential map to an open neighborhood
U(x_{-},x_{+})
of the zero section of
NT_{1}(x_{-},x_{+})
defines a homeomorphism onto its image

r^{exp}_{x_{-},x_{+}}:U(x_{-},x_{+})\to T_{2}(x_{-},x_{+})

which is a fiberwise local diffeomorphism. The compatibility conditions for the metrics imply that

\iota_{x_{-},z,x_{+}}(r^{exp}_{x_{-},z}(a_{1}),r^{exp}_{z,x_{+}}(a_{2}))=r^{exp}_{x_{-},x_{+}}(\underline{\tau^{\prime}_{xzy}}(a_{1},a_{2}))

for all
x_{-}>z>x_{+}
in
\mathcal{C}(H,J)
and all
a_{1}\in T(x_{-},z)
and
a_{2}\in T(z,x_{+})
for which the above maps are defined. Thus, after inductively restricting the neighborhoods
U(x_{-},x_{+})
, these maps define an isomorphism from a restriction of the stabilization of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}(H,J)
to a restriction of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}(H,J)
.
∎

7.8
Continuation map

Let us suppose that we have chosen all the data of the previous paragraphs for a pair of convex nondegenerate Floer data
(H_{-},J_{-})
and
(H_{+},J_{+})
. Given convex continuation data
(H_{s},J_{s})
, we can define continuation map moduli spaces

\mathcal{M}(H_{s},J_{s})(x_{-},x_{+})=\{u:Z\to M:\partial_{s}u+J_{s}\partial_{t}u=\nabla H_{s},\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}u_{s}=x_{\pm}\}

for
x_{\pm}\in Fix(H_{\pm})
. The
the Gromov-Floer bordifications
\overline{\mathcal{M}}(H_{s},J_{s})(x_{-},x_{+})
are compact. If
H_{s}
and
J_{s}
are
C_{k}
-equivariant then there is a loop rotation action of
C_{k}
on the disjoint union of the above spaces over pairs
(x_{-},x_{+})\in Fix(H_{-})\times Fix(H_{+})
. We will explain how to choose ‘perturbation data’ such that these spaces are the underlying spaces of a virtually smooth equivariant flow category which contains stabilizations of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})
as flow subcategories.

Integralization data for continuation maps.

The first bit of data we chose to define
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})
were the auxiliary actions
\mathfrak{A}_{\pm}
and the corresponding integralization data
\bar{\mathfrak{A}_{\pm}}
.

In the construction of Section
7.1
, we relied crucially on the property that the action functional is strictly decreasing along Floer trajectories in order to canonically place markings along the domains of Floer trajectories, thus embedding spaces of Floer trajectories into the thickenings constructed in that section.
Because the action functional does not strictly decrease along continuation map trajectories, we will have to include, as part of the data of an integralization datum for continuation maps, an auxiliary function
c(s)
which which will be used to artificially shift the action functional to a new function which strictly decreases along continuation trajectories. The following lemma bounds how rapidly the action functional can increase along continuation trajectories:

Lemma 29
.

Let
(H_{s},J_{s})
be a convex continuation datum. If
u\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}(H_{s},J_{s})(x_{-},x_{+})
then, as functions of
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\frac{d}{ds}\mathcal{A}_{H_{s}}(u_{s})<\max_{(x,t)\in M\times S^{1}}|\partial_{s}H_{s}(x,t)|

and in particular

\mathcal{A}_{H_{+}}(x_{+})-\mathcal{A}_{H_{-}}(x_{-})<\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\max_{(x,t)\in M\times S^{1}}|\partial_{s}H_{s}(x,t)|

Proof.

This is essentially standard. When
H_{s}
is independent of
s
, we have that
(d/ds)\mathcal{A}_{H_{s}}(u_{s})<0
since
u_{s}
is in this case the gradient flow of this action functional. The only new term that arises in the computation of
(d/ds)\mathcal{A}_{H_{s}}(u_{s})
when
H_{s}
is not independent of
t
is

\int_{0}^{1}-(\partial_{s}H_{s})(u_{s}(t))dt\leq\max_{(x,t)\in M\times S^{1}}|\partial_{s}H_{s}(x)|.

Since
\partial_{s}u_{s}|_{s=s_{0}}
agrees with the vector field along
u_{s_{0}}
corresponding to the gradient flow of
\mathcal{A}_{H_{s_{0}}}
, at
s=s_{0}
, the remanining terms in
((d/ds)\mathcal{A}_{H_{s}}(u_{s}))|_{s=s_{0}}
sum to a strictly negative quantity; thus the bound above proves the claims of the lemma.
∎

Definition 84
.

An
integralization datum
for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
is a tuple
(\mathcal{A}^{cont},\mathfrak{A}_{cont},\{A_{c}\})
such that

(A)

There exist functions
c:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
and constants
C_{\pm}\in\mathbb{R}
and
D>0
such that

•

\mathcal{A}^{cont}=D\mathcal{A}_{H_{s}}+c(s)
as functions
\mathbb{R}\times LX\to\mathbb{R}
;

•

c(s)=C_{\pm}
for
\pm s>>0
;

•

\frac{d}{ds}c(s)<D\max_{(x,t)\in M\times S^{1}}|\partial_{s}H_{s}(x)|
.

(B)

\mathfrak{A}_{cont}\subset\mathbb{R}
can be written as

\mathfrak{A}_{cont}=(\mathfrak{A}_{-}+C_{-})\cup(\mathfrak{A}_{+}+C_{+}),

(100)

with

\max_{A\in\mathfrak{A}_{+}+C_{+}}A<\min_{A\in\mathfrak{A}_{-}+C_{-}}A;
.

(C)

In addition, we have that
(D\mathcal{A}_{H_{\pm}}+C_{\pm},\mathfrak{A}_{\pm}+C_{\pm},)
is an integralization datum for
\mathcal{C}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})
.

(D)

Finally, for every
x_{\pm}\in Fix(H_{\pm})
, we have that the set
\mathfrak{A}(x_{-},x_{+})
contains at least two elements
A_{c}^{-},A_{c}^{+}
such that
A_{c}^{+}>A_{c}>A^{c}_{-}
.

A crucial observation is that Lemma
29
and the condition on
c(s)
implies that for every pair
(x_{-},x_{+})\in Fix(H_{-})\times Fix(H_{+})
, and every continuation map solution
u\in\mathcal{M}(H_{s},J_{s})(x_{-},x_{+})
, we have that

\frac{d}{ds}\mathcal{A}^{cont}(s)<0\text{ for all }s.

(101)

Lemma 30
.

Given
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{\pm}
arbitrary integralization data for
\mathcal{C}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})
, there exists an integralization datum
\bar{A}
for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
such that the integralization data defined in (C) of Definition
84
agree with
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{\pm}
.

Proof.

The set of
s\in\mathbb{R}
where
\frac{d}{ds}\mathcal{A}_{H_{s}}(u_{s})
for
u\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}(H_{s},J_{s})(x_{-},x_{+})
is all contained in the compact subset of
\mathbb{R}
are where
\frac{d}{ds}H_{s}\neq 0
; thus, by compactness
\overline{\mathcal{M}}(H_{s},J_{s})(x_{-},x_{+})
and finiteness of
Fix(H_{\pm})
, by choosing
c(s)
to be a sufficiently large rescaling of any function which has strictly negative derivative over that region will satisfy the first (and most difficult to satisfy) constraint. The remaining constraints together with the choice of
c
characterize
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
.
∎

Shifting integralization data.

Let
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}=(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{A})
be an integralization datum for
\mathcal{C}(H,J)
. For
D>0
and any constant
C
, define

D\bar{\mathfrak{A}}+C=(D\mathcal{A}+C,D\mathfrak{A}+C).

then this is also an integralization datum for
\mathcal{C}(H,J)
which defines the same stratification of the underlying pre-flow category. Similarly, if
\mathfrak{P}
is a regular compatible system of perturbation data for
\mathcal{C}(H<J)
with underlying integralization datum
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
, then, defining
D\mathfrak{P}+C
to be tuple produced from
\mathfrak{P}
by replacing
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
by
D\bar{\mathfrak{A}}+C
we see that this is also a regular system of perturbation data, and the resulting categories
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)
produced from
\mathfrak{P}
and from
D\bar{\mathfrak{A}}+C
are isomorphic after rescaling the codomains of the functions
\delta A
by
D
.

Parameter spaces and stratifications.

To define the parameter spaces for our virtual smoothings of continuation map moduli spaces, we must introduce a variant of
\overline{Conf}_{r}
.

Let
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
be an integralization datum for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
; write

\mathfrak{A}=\mathfrak{A}_{-}\sqcup\mathfrak{A}_{+}

where this is the decomposition (
100
). Choose an
A_{c}
such that
\min\mathfrak{A}_{-}>A_{c}>\max\mathfrak{A}_{+}
; this is possible by (B) of Definition
84
. Write
\underline{\mathfrak{A}}=\mathfrak{A}\cup A_{c}
, with the induced ordering. Write
\underline{\mathfrak{A}}(x)=\{A\in\underline{\mathfrak{A}}:A<\mathcal{A}(x)\}
, where
\mathcal{A}
is the augmented action underlying
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
. We give the pre-flow category
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
the integral action

\bar{A}(x)=\#\underline{\mathfrak{A}}(x)-1.

This is an integral action if we set

S_{\mathcal{C}(x,y)}=\underline{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}(x,y):=\underline{\mathfrak{A}}(x,y)\setminus A_{y}^{+},\text{ where }\underline{\mathfrak{A}}(x,y)=\{A\in\underline{\mathfrak{A}}:\mathcal{A}(x)>A>\mathcal{A}(y)\},

and now
A_{y}^{+}
is the smallest element of
\underline{\mathfrak{A}}=\mathfrak{A}\cup A_{c}
which is greater than
\mathcal{A}(y)
. More concretely, we can write this as

\bar{A}(x)=\bar{A}_{+}(x)\text{ for }x\in Fix(H_{+}),

\bar{A}(x)=\bar{A}_{-}(x)+\#\mathfrak{A}_{+}+1\text{ for }x\in Fix(H_{-}).

(102)

We will now define a open subset
\overline{Conf}^{o}_{\underline{\mathfrak{A}}(x,y)}\subset(\overline{Conf}_{\mathfrak{A}(x,y)})^{\mathbb{R}}
. An element of
(\overline{Conf}_{\mathfrak{A}(x,y)})^{\mathbb{R}}
is an element
a
of
(\overline{Conf}_{\mathfrak{A}(x,y)})
together with an additional marked
s
coordinate, which we will call
s_{A_{c}}
, on one of the components of the universal curve over
a
. The subset
\overline{Conf}^{o}_{\underline{\mathfrak{A}}(x,y)}
consists of those elements such that
(a,s_{A_{c}})
satisfying the property that
a
lies in a stratum where there is a component of the universal curve containing marked points labeled by actions from both
\mathfrak{A}_{+}
and from
\mathfrak{A}_{-}
, and
s_{A_{c}}
is also is on that component.

Notice that we may have that
s_{A_{c}}<s_{\min\mathfrak{A}_{-}}
or
s_{A_{c}}>s_{\max\mathfrak{A}_{+}}
; these are precisely the constraints that would hold if we considered
\overline{Conf}_{\underline{\mathfrak{A}(x,y)}}
instead, and are precisely the constraints that we wish to ignore in our construction. See also the discussion in Figure
9
, which also depicts the corresponding parameter spaces.

Figure 9:
Parameter spaces of curves for continuation map.
When defining global charts for the flow categories corresponding to the continuation map, one must modify the parameter spaces in the way described above. The marked actions from
\mathfrak{A}_{\pm}
are denoted
A^{\pm}_{i}
above. One adds an extra marked
s
-coordinate (labeled
s_{A_{c}}
which is used to ‘center’ the corresponding component of the domain so as to write the continuation map equation; however, that marked point has no component of the obstruction section constraining it to lie on the modified action value
A_{c}
, and moreover it can move anywhere on the corresponding component of the domain, not being required to lie in between
s_{A^{-}_{r}}
and
s_{A_{1}^{+}}
or otherwise. (Indeed, there is no reason that the modified action value in the ‘middle’ of a coninuation map trajectory would be below those two values). A precise description of this space is given in the text. The discussion for continuation map homotopies is similar. Because of condition
D
of Definition
84
there are no breakings of broken continuation trajectories in regions with modified action between
A_{r}^{-}
and
A_{1}^{+}
, one does not need to add corresponding strata to the moduli space. In the stratification, we think of the set organizing the stratification of this space as the spaces in bewteen the subsequent marked
s
-coordiantes points (even though
s_{A_{c}}
may be out of order on a given curve), and one removes an element from the set for every breakpoint (one never removes the elements corresponding to the spaces to the left and right of
s_{A_{c}}
due to Condition (D), thus dealing with the issue regarding the fact that
s_{A_{c}}
may not lie in order).

We now define a stratification of
\overline{Conf}^{o}_{\underline{\mathfrak{A}}(x,y)}
making it into a
\langle\underline{\mathfrak{A}}(x,y)^{\prime}\rangle
-manifold. Recall as before that we identify
\underline{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}(x,y)
with the regions in between the elements of
\underline{\mathfrak{A}}(x,y)
; given an element
\tilde{a}\in\overline{Conf}^{o}_{\underline{\mathfrak{A}}(x,y)}
we put it in the stratum
S(\tilde{a})=\underline{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}(x,y)\setminus S^{c}(\tilde{a})
, where
S^{c}(\tilde{a})
contains the elements corresponding to the regions where there are break-points in between marked coordinates labeled by elements of
\mathfrak{A}(x,y)
. (See Figure
9
.)
Now, for
x>y

\mathcal{C}(H_{t},J_{t})
with
x\in Fix(H_{-})
and
y\in Fix(H_{+})
, we define

{\overline{M}}(x,y)={\overline{M}}(\bar{A}(x),\bar{A}(y))=\overline{Conf}^{o}_{\underline{\mathfrak{A}}(x,y)}.

We define the universal curves over over
\overline{M}(x,y)
to be the pullback of the corresponding universal curves from the first factor.

One has maps
\iota_{xzy}
covered by maps
\iota^{\prime}_{xzy}
identifying universal curves as in (
82
). Systems of perturbation data and their compatibility are defined as earlier but with the new notation. Specifically, given a point
p
in
{\overline{M}}(x,y)^{Z}
, let
\tilde{s}(p)=-\infty
if
p
lies on a component to the right of
s_{A_{c}}
,
\tilde{s}(p)=+\infty
if
p
lies on a component to the left of
s_{A_{c}}
, and otherwise
\tilde{s}(p)
is the value of
s(p)
if we apply an
s
-translation taking
s_{A_{c}}
is to zero. The definition of
T(x_{-},x_{+})
for
x_{\pm}
both in
Fix(H_{-})
or
Fix(H_{+})
are unchanged; however, if
x_{\pm}\in Fix(H_{\pm})
, we define

T(x_{-},x_{+})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c|r}(u,a,v):v\in V^{1}(x_{-},x_{+}),&\partial_{s}u(s,t)+J_{\tilde{s},t}\partial_{t}u(s,t)-\nabla H_{\tilde{s}}(u(s,t))=\lambda_{V}(((s,t),a),v),\\
a\in{\overline{M}}(x,y),u:{\overline{M}}(x,y)^{Z}_{a}\to M&\lim_{s\to-\infty}u_{1}(s,\cdot)=x_{-},\lim_{s\to\infty}u_{r}(s,\cdot)=x_{+}\\
u=u_{1}\cup\ldots\cup u_{r}&\lim_{s\to+\infty}u_{j}(s,\cdot)=\lim_{s\to-\infty}u_{j+1}(s,\cdot),j=1,\ldots,r-1\end{array}\right\}

(103)

where we interpret
J_{\pm\infty}
as
J_{\pm}
.

Inclusions like (
87
) naturally exist with this definition, and there are vector bundles
\underline{V^{1}(x,y)}
defined as in (
89
) when
x
and
y
are fixed points of the same Hamiltonian, and if
x\in Fix(H_{-})
and
y\in Fix(H_{+})
is

V^{1}_{T}(x,y)=V^{1}(x,y)\oplus V^{1}_{can}(x,y),V^{1}_{can}(x,y)=\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{A}(x,y)}.

(104)

Remark 53
.

As before
V^{1}_{can}(x,y)
comes from the
E^{s}
-parameterization
V^{1}_{can}(x)
(
104
). However, note that
\mathfrak{A}(x,y)
differs in general from
\mathfrak{A}(x,y)
by the element
A_{c}
; thus, we will
not
have a component of obstruction bundle corresponding to the marked point
s_{A_{c}}
, which in turn corresponds to the fact that one does not quotient the space of solutions to the continuation equation by an
\mathbb{R}
-action.

The obstruction section, as in all cases, is

\sigma(x,y):T(x,y)\to\underline{V^{1}_{T}(x,y)},\sigma(x,y)(u,a,v)=(v,\delta A(u,a))

where

\delta A(u,a)=(\mathcal{A}(u|_{s_{A}})-A)_{A\in\mathfrak{A}(x,y)}.

As before we have maps like (
88
), and the property (
112
) implies that
\sigma(x_{-},x_{+})^{-1}(0)
is exactly the space of broken continuation map trajectories trajectories
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})(x_{-},x_{+})
. Thus we define a virtual topological flow category

\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})(x,y)=(T(x_{-},x_{+}),V^{1}_{T}(x_{-},x_{+}),\sigma(x_{-},x_{+}))
.

Proposition 19
.

Suppose that we have fixed compatible regular systems of perturbation data
\mathfrak{P}_{\pm}
over
\mathcal{C}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})
. There exists a compatible regular system of perturbation data
\mathfrak{P}
over
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
(with integralization data defined as above) such that the restriction of
\mathfrak{P}
to
\mathcal{C}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})
is a stabilization of
\mathfrak{P}_{\pm}
by an
E^{s}
-parameterizaton equipped with trivial perturbation data.

Proof.

Recall that the parameterizations underlying a compatible system of perturbation data are free. Thus,
\mathfrak{P}_{-}
extends to a compatible system of perturbation data over
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
which restricts to
\mathcal{C}(H_{-},J_{-})
in a unique fashion given the constraing that the underlying
E^{s}
-parameterization of the extension (which we will also denote by
\mathfrak{P}_{-}
) assigns to any element
x\in\mathcal{C}(H_{+},J_{+})
the zero vector space. We can then run the argument in the proof of Proposition
7
twice, each time with the following modifications:

•

The first time, the goal is to extend
\mathfrak{P}_{+}
to a compatible system of perturbation data over
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
(also denoted by
\mathfrak{P}_{+}
) such that for
x>y
in
\mathcal{C}(H_{-},J_{-})
,
\lambda_{V_{\mathfrak{P}_{+}}(x,y)}=0
. Write
\mathcal{E}^{s}_{+}
for the
E
-parameterization underlying
\mathcal{P}_{+}
; this
E
-parameterization automatically extends to
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
(and will be denoted by the same symbol) by requiring that it is built from the zero parameterization only by stabilizing at objects of
\mathcal{C}(H_{+},J_{+})
, and otherwise such that canonical part of the paramerization assigns the zero vector space to all markings in
\underline{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus\mathfrak{A}_{+}
. The goal of this bullet is accomplished by running the induction in Proposition
7
starting at
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{C}(H_{+},J_{+})
with
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}=\mathcal{E}_{+}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{P}}=(\bar{\mathfrak{A}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}|_{\mathcal{P}},\{\lambda_{V_{\mathcal{P}}})
(with notation as in the proof of Proposition
7
); the inductive condition is weakened to the condition that
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{P}}
restricts to
\mathfrak{P}_{+}
along
\mathcal{C}(H_{+},J_{+})
and such that
\lambda_{V_{\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{P}}}(x,y)}=0
for
x,y\in\mathcal{P}\cap\mathcal{C}(H_{-},J_{-})
. At each step of the inner loop if
y\in\mathcal{C}(H_{-},J_{-})
we set
\lambda_{V_{\mathcal{P}_{x},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}}(x,y)=0
and for other
y
we simply do
nothing
when exectuting the inner loop. The final value
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})}
defines the extension
\mathfrak{P}_{+}
.

•

At this point we have the compatible system of perturbation data
\mathfrak{P}_{\pm}=\mathfrak{P}_{-}\oplus\mathfrak{P}_{+}
(where direct sum is defined by taking direct sum of
F^{s}
-parameterizations and direct sum of perturbation data maps
\lambda
) which is regular when restricted to
\mathcal{C}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})
. Thus for any
x\in\mathcal{C}(H_{-},J_{-})
and
y\in\mathcal{C}(H_{+},J_{+})
there is a compact subset
K_{xy}\subset\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})(x,y)
defined by the property that
u\in K
if
D^{\mathfrak{P}_{+}\oplus\mathfrak{P}_{-}}_{u}
is not surjective. We then rerun the entire double induction of Proposition
7
starting at
\mathcal{P}=0
but with the inductive condition in the outer loop being that
\lambda_{V_{\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{P}}}}(x,y)=0
for both
x
and
y
in
\mathcal{C}(H_{-},J_{-})
or in
\mathcal{C}(H_{+},J_{+})
; and also that
D^{\mathfrak{P}_{+}\oplus\mathfrak{P}_{-}}_{u}
is surjective for
u\in K_{xy}
; it is straightforward to modify the inner loop to achieve this condition. After the double induction terminates we set
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})}=:\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
.

We finalize by setting
\mathfrak{P}=\mathfrak{P}_{-}\oplus\mathfrak{P}_{+}\oplus\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
.
∎

We will write
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})
for the topologically-smooth flow categories associated to
\mathfrak{P}_{\pm}
. We have the following proposition showing the smoothings we choose for either of these are compatible with a smoothing for the continuation-map flow category:

Proposition 20
.

The category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})
defined using
\mathfrak{P}
produced by Proposition
19
, is topologically smooth.

Moreover, let
\mathcal{E}_{\pm}
be
E
-parameterizations such that
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})_{\mathcal{E}_{\pm}}
have been given smooth structures. Then there exists an
E^{s}
-parameterization
\mathcal{E}
such that such that
\mathcal{E}|_{\mathcal{C}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})}
is a semi-free stabilization of
\mathcal{E}_{\pm}
by some
\mathcal{E}_{\pm}^{\prime}
, and such that
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})_{\mathcal{E}}
has a smooth structure
with with
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})_{\mathcal{E}}|_{\mathcal{C}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})}=(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})_{\mathcal{E}_{\pm}})_{\mathcal{E}_{\pm}^{\prime}}
as virtually smooth flow categories.

Proof.

The fact that
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})
is topoloogically smooth follows from the corresponding analog of Proposition
10
, which is proven by the same arguments from
[
81
]
. By the conditions that
\mathfrak{P}
satisfies we have that
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})|_{\mathcal{C}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm}})
is indeed a stabilization of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})
by some
E^{s}
-parameterization
\mathcal{E}_{\pm}^{0}
. Extend each of
\mathcal{E}_{\pm}
to
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
by requiring that the extension is built from the zero parameterization by stabilizing only at elements of
\mathcal{C}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})
. Write

\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})=\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})_{\mathcal{E}_{-}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{+}}|_{\mathcal{C}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})};

we have that

\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})=\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})_{\mathcal{E}_{\pm}^{0}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{-}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{+}}=(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})_{\mathcal{E}_{\pm}})_{\mathcal{E}_{\mp}\oplus\mathcal{E}^{0}_{\pm}}

and as such the full subcategories
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})_{\mathcal{C}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})}
are equipped with smooth structures induced from the smooth structures given on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{\pm},J_{\pm})_{\mathcal{E}_{\pm}}
. We now rerun the equivariant double induction in the proof of Proposition
15
on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
, with the proviso that for pairs
(x,y)
(with notation as in the proof of that proposition) for which both
x
and
y
are in
\mathcal{C}(H_{-},J_{-})
or in
\mathcal{C}(H_{+},J_{+})
, we use the smooth structure on
T(x,y)
induced by its presentation as the product of
T_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})}(x,y)
with a vector space. The conclusion of that double induction outputs an
E^{s}
-parameterization
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})}
, and we can set
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{-}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{+}
. ∎

We have now a sufficient number of results to establish the existence of continuation maps between Floer homotopy types associated to Hamiltonian Floer homologies. We combine the relevant results in Section
10.1
.

7.9
Cyclotomic compatibility

In previous sections we have described how to produce
C_{k}
-equivariant virtually smooth flow categories
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})
when
(H,J)
or
(H_{s},J_{s})
are
C_{k}
-invariant. This latter condition of course means e.g. that
(H,J)=(H^{\#k},J^{\#k})
. In this section we explain how to pick perturbation data and smoothings such that the virual smoothings
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H^{\#k},J^{\#k})
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H^{\#k}_{s},J^{\#k}_{s})
are all compatible
as
k
varies
. We will only work out the case of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H^{\#k}_{s},J^{\#k}_{s})
since it contains the case of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H^{\#k},J^{\#k})
as a subproblem.

Specifically, we prove the following

Proposition 21
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}=(\bar{\mathfrak{A}},\mathfrak{F},\{\lambda_{V_{\mathfrak{P}}(x,y)}\})
be a compatible regular system of perturbation data for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
such that
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})
(defined using
\mathfrak{P}
has a smooth structure; if
(H_{s},J_{s})
are
C_{\ell}
-invariant we assume that all the associated data are
C_{\ell}
-equivariant as well. Suppose also that
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
satisfies the conditions in Lemma
33
below. Fix
k
a natural number greater than one. There exists a compatible
C_{k\ell}
-equivariant regular system of perturbation data
\mathfrak{P}_{k}=(\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{k},\mathfrak{F}_{k},\{\lambda_{V_{\mathfrak{P}_{k}}(x,y)}\})
for
\mathcal{C}(H^{\#k}_{s},J^{\#k}_{s})
such that

•

The flow category
\mathcal{C}(H^{\#k}_{s},J^{\#k}_{s})^{C_{k}}
(with the stratification induced from
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{k}
is a restratification of
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
;

•

The topologically smooth category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H^{\#k}_{s},J^{\#k}_{s})
defined using
\mathfrak{P}_{k}
has a
C_{k\ell}
smooth structure, which

•

Restricts to a smooth structure on the virtually smooth
C_{k\ell}/C_{k}
-equivariant flow category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H^{\#k}_{s},J^{\#k}_{s})^{C_{k}}
, which in turn is isomorphic to a stabilization ( by an
E^{s}
-parameterization) of a restratification
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})
as a
C_{\ell}=C_{k\ell}/C_{k}
-equivariant virtually smooth flow category.

We prove this proposition by combining several lemmas proven below.

Proof of Proposition
21
.

Combine Lemmas
33
,
34
,
35
, and
36
.
∎

Shifting constants and a-priori bounds on actions of periodic points.

Lemma 31
.

Given a an admissible symplectic manifold
(M,\omega)
and a Hamiltonian function
H:M\times S^{1}\to\mathbb{R}
, there is a constant
C_{H}
depending only on
M
and
H
such that
\mathcal{A}_{H^{\#k}}(Fix(H^{\#k}))\subset[kC_{H},-kC_{H}]
.

Proof.

Pick a compatible almost complex structure
J
on
M
. The action functional is

\mathcal{A}(\gamma)=\int_{0}^{1}\lambda(\gamma^{\prime})-H(\gamma).

Recall that we have that
\gamma^{\prime}=-\nabla H
; since
M
is compact we have the bound

\mathcal{A}(\gamma)\leq\|\lambda\|_{C^{0}}\|H\|_{C^{1}}\}+\|H\|_{C^{0}}\leq C_{1}\|H\|_{C^{1}};

for some constant
C_{1}
, where we write
\|H\|_{C^{1}}=\sup_{t\in S^{1}}\|H_{t}\|_{C^{1}}
and similarly for
\|H\|_{C^{0}}
. Notice that
C_{1}
is independent of
H
; the proposition then follows from the fact that
\|H^{\#k}\|_{C^{1}}=k\|H\|_{C^{1}}
.
∎

Compatibly choosing integralization data.

In order to proceed we will need to change the integralization data for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
. The integralization data will change in a comparatively minor way: it will be
extended
.

Definition 85
.

Let
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}=(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{A})
. and
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}=(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathfrak{A}^{\prime})
be a pair of integralization data for
\mathcal{C}(H,J)
or for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
(in the latter case we add on the extra quantities
\{A_{c}\}
and
\{A^{\prime}_{c}\}
to the respective tuples to the tuple. We say that
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
is an
extension
of
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
if

\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{A}\sqcup\mathfrak{A}_{new},

and in the case that we are discussing
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
we also have that
A_{c}=A^{\prime}_{c}
.

On the way to proving Lemma
21
, we will prove the following helpful Lemma as well:

Lemma 32
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a perturbation datum for
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})
with underlying integralization datum
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
. Let
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
be an extension of
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
.
Then there is a perturbation datum
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
with underlying integralization datum
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
such that the virtual topological smoothing
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
associated to
\mathfrak{P}
has agrees with a restriction of a restratification of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})_{\mathfrak{P}}
, the topological smoothing associated to
\mathfrak{P}
. The same holds with
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
replaced by
\mathcal{C}(H,J)
.

Remark 54
.

As before, there is an action of orientation-preserving affine rescalings of
\mathbb{R}
on integralization data for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
namely,
C\bar{\mathfrak{A}}+D=(C\mathcal{A}+D,C\mathfrak{A}+D,\{CA_{c}+D\})
, and given a perturbation datum
\mathfrak{P}
with integralization data
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
, there is a corresponding perturbation datum
C\mathfrak{P}+D
with underlying integralization datum
C\bar{\mathfrak{A}}+D
defining a virtual smoothing which is canonically isomorphic to that defined via
\mathfrak{P}
.

Remark 55
.

In Proposition
21
, the integralization datum
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{k}
will satisfy the property that if we think of it as an integralization datum for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}^{\#k})^{C_{k}}
, it will have the form
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{k}=k\bar{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
where
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
is an extension of
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
under the identification
\mathcal{C}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}^{\#k})^{C_{k}}\simeq\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
. The lemma below explains how to construct
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{k}
from
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
.

Remark 56
.

Note that
\mathcal{A}_{H^{\#k}}(\gamma^{k})
for a
C_{k}
-invariant loop
\gamma^{k}(t)=\gamma(kt)
agrees with
k\mathcal{A}_{H}(\gamma)
. Thus, implicitly in the remark above in the Lemma below, we are using the fact that if we identify
\mathcal{C}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}^{\#k})^{C_{k}}
with
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
, then the integralization datum
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
corresponds to the integralization datum
k\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}^{\#k})^{C_{k}}
.

Lemma 33
.

Let
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}=(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{A},\{A_{c}\})
be an integralization datum for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
.
Write
\mathcal{A}=D\mathcal{A}_{H_{s}}+c(s)
with
c(s)\to C_{\pm}
as
s\to\pm\infty
. Suppose that

C_{-}-C_{+}>DC_{H_{-}}+DC_{H_{+}}+\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}D\max_{(x,t)\in M\times S^{1}}|\partial_{s}H_{s}|ds

(105)

and

A_{c}\in(C_{-}-DC_{H_{-}},C_{+}+DC_{H_{+}}).

(106)

where
C_{H_{\pm}}
are the constants defined in Lemma
31
.
Identify the objects of
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
with a subset of the objects of
\mathcal{C}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}^{\#k})
via iteration.
Then there exists a set
\mathfrak{A}^{k}_{n}\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus k\mathfrak{A}
such that

\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{k}=(D\mathcal{A}_{H_{s}^{\#k}}+kc(s),k\mathfrak{A}\cup\mathfrak{A}^{k}_{n},\{kA_{c}\})

is an integralization datum for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}^{\#k})
. Moreover, viewing
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{k}
as an integralization datum for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}^{\#k})^{C_{k}}\simeq\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
, the integral action on
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
induced via
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{k}
is a restratification of the integral action induced on
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
via
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
.

Proof.

Note first that
H_{s}^{\#k}(x,t)=kH_{s}(x,kt)
, so

\max_{(x,y)\in M\times S^{1}}|\partial_{s}H_{s}^{\#k}(x,t)|=k\max_{(x,y)\in M\times S^{1}}|\partial_{s}H_{s}(x,t)|.

Therefore,
kc(s)
satisfies Condition
A
from Definition
84
for
H_{s}^{\#k}
. In order for the set
\mathfrak{A}_{cont,new}
to exist, it is sufficient to have that

\min_{x\in\mathcal{C}(H_{-},J_{-})}D\mathcal{A}_{H_{-}^{\#k}}(x)+kC_{-}>\max_{x\in\mathcal{C}(H_{+},J_{+})}\mathcal{A}_{H_{-}^{\#k}}(x)+kC_{+};

this is guaranteed by the condition (
105
) as well as Lemma
31
. The last statement is elementary.
∎

Compatibly choosing perturbation data.

We will now explain how to use the integralization data
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{k}
produced by Lemma
33
to extend perturbation data
\mathfrak{P}
for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
to perturbation data
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}{\#k})
which are compatible with
\mathfrak{P}
in an appropriate fashion. Before we can explain the compatibility conditions, we must first explain some geometric statements about the change in the parameter spaces
{\overline{M}}(x,y)
associated to
\mathcal{C}(H_{k},J_{k})^{C_{k}}
induced by the extension
k\bar{\mathfrak{A}}\to\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{k}
. This process is geometrically rather elementary, as one can see by “adding new marked points” to Figure
7
.

Write
{\overline{M}}(x,y)
for the parameter spaces associated by
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
to
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
, and write
{\overline{M}}_{k}(x,y)
for the parameter spaces associated by
\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{k}
to
\mathcal{C}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}^{\#k})
. Given an object
x\in\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
, write
x^{k}
for the corresponding object of
\mathcal{C}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}^{\#k})
. Then thinking of
k\bar{\mathfrak{A}}
as an integralization datum for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}^{\#k})^{k}
, this data assigns parameter spaces to
(x^{k},y^{k})
which are canonically isomorphic to
{\overline{M}}(x,y)
.

Recall that

{\overline{M}}(x,y)=\overline{Conf}^{c}_{\underline{\mathfrak{A}}(x,y)},{\overline{M}}_{k}(x,y)=\overline{Conf}^{c}_{\underline{\mathfrak{A}_{k}}(x,y)}.

There open submanifold
U(x^{k},y^{k})\subset\overline{Conf}^{c}_{\underline{\mathfrak{A}_{k}}(x^{k},y^{k})}
corresponding to those curves for which if we removed the marked points labeled by elements of
\mathfrak{A}_{k}\setminus k\mathfrak{A}
, we would get a stable domain. Thus there is a projection

m:U(x^{k},y^{k})\to{\overline{M}}(x,y).

(107)

We have that

\iota_{x^{k}z^{k}y^{k}}(U(x^{k},z^{k})\times U(z^{k},y^{k}))=U(x^{k},y^{k}).

(108)

Write
U(x^{k},y^{k})^{Z}
for the preimage of
U(x^{k},y^{k})
in the total space of of the universal curve to
{\overline{M}}(x^{k},y^{k})^{Z}
.

Note that the definition of a compatible system of perturbation data (Definition
75
) continues to make sense if the maps
\lambda_{V^{1}(x,y)}
(in the notation of that definition) are only partially defined over the
U(x^{k},y^{k})
, i.e.

Writing
\mathfrak{P}=(\bar{\mathfrak{A}},\mathcal{F},\{\lambda_{V_{\mathcal{F}}(x,y)}\}
for a compatible regular system of perturbation data for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
, we can define the data

\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}_{0}=(\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{k},\mathcal{F}_{0},\{\lambda_{V_{\mathcal{F}}(x^{k},y^{k})}\}_{x,y\in Fix(H)}),\lambda_{V_{\mathcal{F}}(x^{k},y^{k})}(a,v)=\lambda_{V_{\mathcal{F}}(x,y)}(m(a),v).

(109)

The above data is similar to a compatible system of perturbation data, but instead is only a partially-defined such system. We explain the notation.
The
F^{s}
-parameterization
\mathcal{F}_{0}
is the
F^{s}
-parameterization obtained from
\mathcal{F}
by ensuring that the following
E^{s}
-parameterization
\mathcal{E}_{0}=((\mathcal{E}_{0})_{f}\oplus(\mathcal{E}_{0})_{c})
. First, we set
V^{*}_{(\mathcal{E}_{0})_{f}}(x)=0
for all
x\in Fix(H^{\#k})\setminus Fix(H)
, otherwise
V_{(\mathcal{E}_{0})_{f}}(x^{k})=V_{\mathcal{E}_{f}}(x)
, where
\mathcal{E}_{f}
is free part of the
E^{s}
-parameterization
\mathcal{E}_{f}
corresponding to
\mathcal{F}
; with the
C_{\ell k}
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{E}_{0}
coming from the
C_{\ell}
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{E}
via the quotient map
C_{\ell k}/C_{k}\to C_{\ell}
. Second, we require that the vector spaces associated to all elements of
S_{\bar{A}_{k}}\setminus\rho(S_{k\bar{A}})
by the canonical part
(\mathcal{E}_{0})_{c}
of
\mathcal{E}_{0}
parameterization are zero (Definition
64
), while the vector spaces associated to the elements of
\rho(S_{k\bar{A}})
are the vector spaces associated to the corresponding elements of
S_{\bar{A}}
by the canonical part
\mathcal{E}_{c}
of

. With this choice,
V_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}(x^{k},y^{k})=V_{\mathcal{F}}(x,y)
, and the formulae in (

) make sense, with the proviso that the bundle maps
\lambda_{V_{\mathcal{F}}(x^{k},y^{k})}
are defined only over

, and because

is not defined for non-
C_{k}
invariant

or

.

We now have the following

Lemma 34
.

There exist open subsets with compact closure
U^{c}(x^{k},y^{k})\subset U(x^{k},y^{k})
satisfying (
108
) with
U(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
replaced with

\overline{U^{c}(x^{k},y^{k})}\cap\overline{im\iota_{x^{z}y^{k}}}=\emptyset\text{ for any }z\in Fix(H_{s}^{\#k})\setminus Fix(H_{s})

(110)

such that if any perturbation
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=(\bar{\mathfrak{A}}_{k},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\{\lambda_{V_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}(x,y)})
for
\mathcal{C}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}^{\#k})

(see Definition

) and

\lambda_{V_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(x^{k},y^{k})}}(a,v)=\lambda_{V_{\mathcal{F}}(x,y)}(m(a),v)

and

then there is a virtual topological flow category from a restriction of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}^{\#k})_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{C_{k}}
to a restriction of
(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})_{r}
, where

is the restratification associated to the extension of integralization data

Proof.

Write
T(x,y)
for the thickenings associated to
\mathfrak{P}
,
T(x,y)_{r}
for the thickenings associated to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})_{r}
, and

for the thickenings associated to
P^{\prime}
. We will first produce maps

q_{xy}:\mathcal{C}(x,y)\to U(x,y),\iota_{xyz}\circ(q_{xy}\times q_{yz})=q_{xz}\circ\bar{f}_{xyz};

each
q_{xy}
will extend to some open neighborhood of the zero locus in

, and we will define

to be some system of open subsets of

with compact closure satisfying (

) such that

(such a system always exists by a straightforward inductive argument). Given

, we have a corresponding element
(u,a,v)\in T(x,y)
. By condition
112
there unique collection of additional marked
s
-coordinates
\{s_{A}\}_{A\in\mathfrak{A}_{k}\setminus k\mathfrak{A}}
that we can add to
{\overline{M}}{M}(x,y)^{Z}_{a}
such that
\mathcal{A}(s_{A})=A
. The corresponding marked curve defines the element
q_{xy}(u)\in U(x,y)
. The same definition clearly extends to a map

; we will explain how to extend it to a neighborhood

of

in

Namely, we shrink the second factor to
(-\epsilon,\epsilon)^{\mathfrak{A}_{k}\setminus k\mathfrak{A}}
, where
\epsilon
is smaller than a third of the minimum distance between any two elements of
\mathfrak{A}_{k}\cup\mathcal{A}(Ob(\mathcal{C}(H^{\#k}_{s},J^{\#k}_{s}))
; and we define

to be element corresponding to marking

with additional marked
s
-coordinates
\{s_{A}\}_{A\in\mathfrak{A}_{k}\setminus k\mathfrak{A}}
such that
A-\mathcal{A}(u_{s_{A})}=a_{j}
. We can always find such a set of points when

is chosen as above, and again by condition (
112
) this choice is unique (see Figure
7
); so we have extended

as desired. By the fact that the projection

was a topological submersion, we see that

is a topological submersion as well; in particular
q(T(x,y)_{r}^{o})
is open.

Let us now pick

as specified previously with the additional condition that
U^{c}(x,y)\subset q(T(x,y)_{r}^{o})
as well as the condition (
110
) (which is straightforward since
q(T(x,y)_{r}^{o})\cap\overline{im\iota_{x^{k}zy^{k}}}
for the newly introduced objects

) and let replace the sets

by their subsets

; this defines the desired restriction of
(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s},J_{s})_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})_{r}
. The desired restriction of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H_{s}^{\#k},J_{s}^{\#k})_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{C_{k}}
is given by taking the thickenings to be the subsets given by the preimages of

under the projections of the thickenings to
{\overline{M}}_{k}(x,y)
.

Suppose now that
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
exists as in the lemma. The desired homeomorphism on thickenings is

T(x,y)_{r}^{o}\ni(u,a,v,(a_{j}))\mapsto(u_{q(a)},q(a),v)=(u^{\prime},b,w)\in T_{k}(x^{k},y^{k}).

(111)

where
u_{q(a)}
is the composition of
u
with identification
{\overline{M}}_{k}{M}(x,y)^{Z}_{q(a)}\to{\overline{M}}{M}(x,y)^{Z}_{a}
obtained by dropping the marked

coordinates added by

. This is certainly a continuous map; to see that it is a homeomorphism, we note that since

and

, we know the perturbation of the Floer equation that

solves as well as its domain; but since the perturbation functions are pulled back under projection under
\pi
, in fact
u^{\prime}
it comes from a unique

, with the location of the extra markings exactly specified by
a_{j}
. This discussion defines an inverse map. These homeomorphsims extend to an isomorphism of virtual topological flow categories by requiring that the bundle maps covering the above homeomorphisms simply act by identity on the fiber.
∎

Finally, we have

Lemma 35
.

A perturbation
\mathfrak{P}^{k}
as in Lemma
34
exists.

Proof.

We rerun the argument proving Proposition
7
twice, with minor modifications similar to the proof of Proposition
19
:

•

We rerun the double induction of Proposition
7
, first while fixing
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}=\mathcal{E}|_{\mathcal{P}}
and
V_{\mathcal{P};\mathcal{Q}}(x,y)=V_{\mathcal{F}}|_{\mathcal{P}}(x,y)
where
\mathcal{F}
. As we run this induction, we always set perturbation functions so that they satisfy the condition

\lambda_{V_{\mathcal{P};\mathcal{Q}}(x^{k},y^{k})}(a,v)=\lambda_{V_{\mathcal{F}}(x,y)}(m(a),v)\text{ for }a\in U^{c}(x,y);

with
U^{c}(x,y)
as in Lemma
34
above; such choices exist by (
110
).

•

The second time we rerun the induction to stabilize the previously chosen perturbation datum by a new perturbation datum
\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{not-inv}
to a regular perturbation datum, such that the underlying
F
-parametriztaion
\mathcal{F}^{not-inv}
of
\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{not-inv}
satisfies
(\mathcal{F}^{not-inv})^{C_{p}}=0
; this is possible because the previously chosen perturbation datum already makes all the
C_{k}
-invariant Floer trajectories regular.

∎

Proof of Lemma
32
.

One follows precisely the same argument as the one above, but replacing
{\overline{M}}_{k}(x,y)
with
{\overline{M}}^{\prime}(x,y)
, the parameter spaces associated to
\mathcal{C}(H_{s},J_{s})
(or to
\mathcal{C}(H,J)
) by the integralization data

\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
everywhere, removing all mention of

; where the condition (
